## Before you hand this over

Run these and **print the result of each**. A check whose result is not
written down did not happen, and a principle in a bullet list does not bind —
this is the same rule stated as work.

**Fix first, in the copy itself, then report.** You cannot go back and edit
what you already wrote, so what you print above must already be corrected. A
fix described under an uncorrected line leaves the wrong words where people
read from.

**1 · TIME.** Name every month, season and holiday you wrote. Beside each,
confirm it agrees with the date at the top. A month that is not the current
one is a defect unless it is plainly historical — substitute the real one and
report it fixed. "The bank said July" is not a defence.

**2 · SOURCE.** For every concrete specific — an age, a number, a duration, a
named behaviour, a quoted line — name the file it came from **and tag what
kind of language it is**: `VERBATIM` (a real person's recorded words) ·
`AVATAR` (a documented behaviour in the profile's phrasing) · `ANGLE` (a line
a marketer drafted) · `FILE` (a product or offer fact). These files record the
difference deliberately; flattening it presents a copywriter's invention as
something a customer said. Anything untraceable is removed or rewritten.

Then one line: how many specifics are `VERBATIM`, and which lines carry
`ANGLE` language. A piece resting mostly on `ANGLE` is not disqualified — it
is a piece whose realness has not been demonstrated, and whoever reads it
should be told that plainly.

## THE JOKE LOCK

A funny opening works because of its joke, and the joke has a shape just like
the line does. Before filling any blank, read the doctrine read's `HUMOR:` line
and the record's opening beat, and print:

`THE JOKE: <none — or how it works, in one line: what is said, what is really
true, and the gap between them that makes it funny>`

Name the MECHANISM, not the words. Shapes a mechanism can take, as examples of
shape only: *an impossible, instant change stated with a straight face*;
*a comparison to a famous face nobody could literally become*; *a deadpan
understatement of an obvious disaster*; *a mock-serious warning about something
trivial*. If the HUMOR line is `none` and the opening plays straight, the
opening stays straight unless the room plants a joke. Straight is the default. A straight source stays straight unless the culture read or the culture map gives a receipt AND the piece is not a testimonial, a before/after, a founder story, a demonstration, or about pain, health or money. A straight source may take a planted joke only when it has a receipt: a meme, format or reference the culture read or the avatar's culture map shows this audience already laughs at. Name the receipt. A joke the writer invented is still a finding.
When the culture read or the culture map gives you one, print `THE JOKE:
PLANTED — <how it works, in one line> · <receipt, quoted from the culture read
or the culture map's humor section>` and every rule below applies to it as if the source had
made it, except that VERSION 0 stays the source's own straight opening. When
neither gives you one, print `THE JOKE: none` and skip this section. Nothing on
the culture map's off-limits line is ever planted.

When there is a joke:

1. **The joke is locked like a fixed word.** Every version, control included,
   carries the same mechanism. A version that loses it has broken the format,
   however true its fills are.
2. **Name the blank the joke lives in** — `JOKE BLANK: [NAME]` — the blank
   whose fill carries the exaggeration, the understatement or the reversal.
3. **Fill the joke blank with a fresh instance of the same joke — never a
   literal benefit.** An exaggeration is filled with another exaggeration of
   the same size; an understatement with another understatement. Keep: no joke on the proof or the offer. Allow: the twist beat may be the product doing its job, shown, not claimed. A plain beat with the proof always follows it.
   So the fill may be the product as the punchline — shown in the frame doing
   what it does — but never the product's result stated as a claim: stating it
   flatly turns the joke into a claim and the laugh is gone. When a fill puts
   the product in the twist, print under that version `PROOF FOLLOWS: <the
   body beat, by its label in the baseline, that proves it plainly>`; if the
   baseline has no such beat, write `PROOF FOLLOWS: [SLOT: plain proof beat
   after the hook]` so the body pass writes one. The hook's job is the laugh
   that makes the viewer watch the body to find out what is really true.
4. **The fill comes from the room, not the writer.** Take its reference world
   from the language rows (the famous faces, tropes and in-words the avatar's
   room already uses — rows tagged `taste`, `in-word`, `subculture` where they
   exist), from the doctrine read's REFERENCE WORLD line, from the culture
   read below and from the avatar's culture map (its humor section: laughs at · tropes · creators).
   Cite it like any other fill. A joke the room does not make is a joke about
   the room.
5. **The truth test still holds, one level down.** A joke is not a claim — an
   impossible exaggeration is heard as a joke, so it needs no proof. What must
   stay true is what the joke points at: the change the body shows. A joke
   fill that points at a change the body never shows is false, and is replaced.
6. **The five variations never use the same instance twice**, the way the main
   blank never takes the same fill twice.

---

## Before you hand these over — print this

Checks. Run them, print the result of each, and fix what fails before
you return anything. A rule stated in prose does not bind.

**THE FORMAT TEST.** Print the FORMAT LINE, then `LINE V0` under it. Say
whether every fixed word is there, in order, unchanged; if not, rewrite the
control into the format and print it again, corrected. Do the same for the
card. The variations are not held to the format — THE DIVERSITY TEST checks
them.

**THE TRUTH TEST.** For each of the six, take every fill and check it against
its ELEMENTS line: is every element it must hold true of it? Say so in one line
per fill, naming the elements. A fill that makes any later beat
false is replaced and printed again, corrected.

**THE DIVERSITY TEST.** Print a table, one row per version V0–V5: TAKE · the
first five words of the LINE · the SCROLL event in a few words · the THUMB in a
few words · the bank row behind it. Then read down each column. **Any two
rows a viewer would take for the same opening — the same moment, the same
first-frame event, the same thumbnail, or one sentence with a word swapped —
fail**: rewrite the later one as a different take, from a different row, and
print the corrected table. The control is the format; a variation that reads
as the control with a word swapped fails too.

**THE RUNG TEST.** The reading names the entry level and quotes that level's
`must_not`. Copy that quoted must-not here, then take all six openings —
control included — one at a time, and say whether it breaks it. **A hook that
names the price, the product or the direct claim earlier than its entry level
allows is a defect, not a style choice**, and it is the failure this test
exists for: the line reads perfectly well and is simply invisible to the
reader it was written for.

Two things this test is not. It is not licence to rewrite the control — if
version 0 breaks the must-not, that is a finding about the source's own
entry level against ours, printed as a flag and left standing. And it is not
a ban on the words themselves later in the asset; it is about where the
argument is allowed to BEGIN.

Any variation that breaks its entry level's must-not is rewritten to enter a
rung lower and printed again, corrected. Print the must-not, the six
verdicts, and every rewrite.

Print all four lists. A check whose result is not written down did not happen.

**THE PORTRAIT TEST — the sixth list, printed only when the entry level is
`unaware`.** For each of the six openings, control included, fill three slots:
`IN FRAME:` the single thing the camera is on · `IN THE LINE:` the noun the
sentence is about · `VERDICT:` portrait | symptom | category | product. Any
opening whose verdict is not `portrait` is rewritten to enter on the person
and printed again, corrected — the control is the one exception: it stays as
the source had it and carries the flag. An opening that shows the symptom
"because it is relatable" is the exact failure this test exists for.

**THE STAGE TEST — the fifth list.** The rung test says where the argument may
*begin*; this one says what it must *lead with*. `{position}` names the
`MARKET STAGE` and `LEADS WITH`. Copy both here. Then take all six openings,
control included, and say in a few words what each one leads with — a plain
claim, an enlarged claim, a mechanism, an enlarged mechanism, or identification
alone — and whether that is what the stage calls for. **An opening that leads
with something this market has already discounted is a hook the reader has
already scrolled past**, however well it reads: at *claims exhausted* a plain
promise is invisible; at *exhausted field* a mechanism is one more pitch.

The control is exempt from rewriting — if version 0 fails, print it as a
flag about the source's market against ours and leave it standing. A variation
that fails is rewritten to lead with what `LEADS WITH` names, built from the
same bank rows, and printed again corrected. A hook does not have to *say* the
mechanism to lead with it — a scroll stopper that shows the how counts; say
which channel carries it. Where the reader's dominant word for the problem
is in `PROBLEM WORD`, every hook uses it; a `NEVER` word in any opening is a
defect, fixed and reported.

Print the stage, its leads-with, the six verdicts, and every rewrite.

**THE JOKE TEST.** Print `THE JOKE:` again. If it is `none`, print `JOKE TEST:
none` and move on. Otherwise, for each of the six, one line: the joke-blank
fill · `LANDS` (the same mechanism, a fresh instance, from the room) or `FLAT`
(a literal benefit stated as a claim, a repeated instance, or the mechanism
lost) · why, in a few words. A fill that shows the product doing its job as the
twist LANDS only with its `PROOF FOLLOWS:` line printed. Every `FLAT` variation
is rewritten with a new instance and printed again, corrected. The control is
the source's own joke and stays as it is.

**A planted joke on a straight source.** If `THE JOKE:` reads `PLANTED`, print
`PLANTED JOKE RECEIPT:` with the line of the culture read or the culture map it
came from, quoted. A straight source may take a planted joke only when it has a receipt: a meme, format or reference the culture read or the avatar's culture map shows this audience already laughs at. Name the receipt. A joke the writer invented is still a finding.
A planted joke with no receipt is removed from every version and printed as
`FINDING: invented joke — <the joke>`; the variations go back to straight. The
control is the source's straight opening and is never given the joke.

**THE STORY TEST.** `{story}` names this run's story and its teller. At least one
hook in the set fills the format's blanks **from that story**: the teller's own
words for the problem, landing in one of the `OPENS IN` moments, in the teller's
register from `VOICE` — the format itself unchanged. It still passes every
other test above. Mark it `STORY OPENER` in the set. If `{story}` is `[UNFILLED]` or
no story was picked, print `STORY TEST: no story` and skip it.


## THE TARGET AWARENESS — Variation video runs only

TARGET AWARENESS: {target_awareness}
SECTIONS THIS LEVEL CARRIES: {branch_sections}

If the two lines above are empty, this is not a Variation video run: skip
this section, and everything above is the whole job.

If they are filled, the source is a proven ad of ours and the baseline
injection is its own script, unchanged — VERSION 0 of the whole variation
tree. This run is ONE awareness level of that ad: the target above. Only
awareness moves. The format, the product, the cast and every part of the
structure the sections line does not touch stay exactly as the baseline has
them.

What changes for the hooks, and nothing else:

1. **Open at the target level.** The six hooks open where the TARGET level
   may begin, not where the reading says the source enters. Read the target's
   row in the awareness levels above — what that viewer knows, what the ad
   must and must not do, where it may begin — and open there. Nothing the
   target level must not do appears in any of the six openings.
2. **The format lock holds for the control.** Find the FORMAT LINE, CARD and
   FRAME from the source exactly as the lock says. Every fixed word of VERSION
   0 stays; only its blanks are refilled — with what the target level's
   opening is about.
3. **VERSION 0 at this level.** When the target is the level the source enters
   on, VERSION 0 is the source's own opening, word for word. At any other
   level, VERSION 0 is the source's own fills moved to the target level with
   the fewest words changed that the level demands — the nearest version of
   the proven hook at this level.
4. **The five variations** are five different takes at the target level, every
   rule above unchanged.
5. **A level the format cannot reach.** If a fixed word itself does something
   the target level must not do, print `LEVEL CONFLICT: <the fixed word> · <the
   must-not it breaks>` directly under the FORMAT lines, write the six as close
   to the level as the fixed words allow, and never change a fixed word to get
   there. That conflict is the owner's call.
6. Print, directly under the heading of section 1: `TARGET LEVEL: <level>` and
   `SOURCE LEVEL: <the level the reading gives>`.


## LANGUAGE USED — the last thing you print

LANGUAGE USED:
- <row id> | <the words as they appear in what you wrote>

One line for every language row above whose words you carried into what you
wrote. The id is the one in [brackets] beside the row. A row you read and did
not use is not listed. If you used none: `LANGUAGE USED: none`. The machine
reads this block to mark those rows as used in this asset, so the next run
reaches for words we have not said yet — list only what you actually used.

## ABSURD, SHOCKING, HYPER-SPECIFIC — WRITTEN FOR REACH (V24, 2026-10-21)

(the owner, 2026-10-21: "what makes them stand out on feeds is the absurdity. it has nothing to do with just claims and brand approval … we need to maximize reach / impressions, the shock value absurdity and hyper specific depictions are what make AI content acceptable".) This is content marketing: the job of every line and every picture it describes is to stop a thumb and
earn the next second. On both lanes, in every format:

- **Absurd on purpose.** The demonstration goes over the top: the problem shown bigger, grosser and stranger than
  life (an oversized model of it at the lens, the remedy doing something visibly dramatic), the way the feeds'
  best AI reels do. Never the polite, average version of a moment.
- **Hyper-specific depictions.** Every picture a line calls for is described exactly — its size against something
  we know, its texture, its colour, its smell or sound where it shows, what it does on screen. "A <visible sign of the problem>" is
  not a depiction; "a coin-sized <visible sign of the problem> with a grey root under it, lifting off like wet paper" is.
- **Shock is the hook.** The first line and the first picture land something the viewer has not seen and cannot
  look away from, at least as hard as the swipe's.
- This is judged by reach, not by brand taste. Never soften a line, a claim or a picture to make it more polite
  (claims stay bold, V20). The facts that keep it true stay: the right product, its real ingredients, the swipe's
  structure, the ask word for word.

# Writing v2 · pass B — Placement + Expansion + Close

This is one pass with 3 parts. Write every part, in order, in ONE answer.
Each part begins with its marker line, printed exactly as shown and alone on
its line — the machine splits your answer on these lines:

=== PART 1: PLACEMENT ===
=== PART 2: EXPANSION ===
=== PART 3: CLOSE ===

A later part reads the earlier parts of THIS answer where its rules say
"given above" / "your PART n". Inputs are given once, at their first mention;
a later mention that says "(given in full above)" means the same block.

## THE SWIPE'S FORMAT — declared at teardown, never re-guessed (v2)

The teardown already decided what kind of swipe this is. Write inside it:

{declared_layers}

Never re-classify the swipe, never call it a different format, never switch
who carries it. If something below seems to ask you to decide the format,
the answer is the one above.

## THE LANE — paid or organic, and it changes only the ask (2026-10-21)

{ask_lane}

Organic and paid are the same video. The owner, 2026-10-20: *"Our organic and
paid content should be indistinguishable. The only core difference is really
just the ask and the way that we gradualize that sales pitch."* So the lane
above changes exactly two things, and nothing else:

1. **The last ask.** Paid: tap the button below and buy. Organic: comment the
   word (the one word the lane line names), or follow. The last thing said and
   the last thing on screen is that ask, and only that ask.
2. **How gradually the pitch arrives.** Organic lets the story carry longer
   before the product's first claim; paid may bring it sooner. The ORDER of the
   argument never changes.

The job, the product, where the product enters, every claim and the finish are
the same on both lanes. Claims stay as bold as the brand files allow on both
(the owner, 2026-10-20: "don't worry about over claiming at this point it's
content"). An organic piece is never a piece without the product, and a paid
piece is never a different video with a button added.

**On the organic lane the close is the turn and the ask — no offer stack** (the owner, 2026-10-21: "i'm going for
real binge watch content man, nothing sterile at all"). After the remedy, the product's turn and the lane's ask,
word for word, and nothing else: no bundle or multi-buy, no dosing or usage schedule, no guarantee text, no
label-style statistics ("85 in 100…"). The product, the mechanism and the claims stay — as bold as the brand files
allow. The paid lane keeps the offer.



---

=== PART 1: PLACEMENT ===

## PART 1 — PLACEMENT: the rules for this part

Here is the target awareness for this run — filled only on a Variation video run, empty everywhere else: {target_awareness}
Here are the sections this awareness level carries, from the awareness map — filled only on a Variation video run, empty everywhere else: {branch_sections}
Here are the five awareness levels, each with what it must and must not do:
{awareness_levels}

Here is a baseline injection — our brand substituted into a format that
performs: {baseline_injection}

Here is the teardown record it came from: {teardown_record}
Here is its replication spec: {replication_spec}
Here is the product: {product_file}
Here is our objection bank: {objection_bank}
Here is the reading of the source — its lane, its opening beat and its
awareness entry: PART 1 (READING) of the hook-pass output, given just below with the hook set.
Here is the hook set that will open our version: {hook_set}
Here is our brand's declared position — its LINE, SPINE, MECHANISM, the MARKET STAGE it sells
into and what that stage LEADS WITH, as labelled slots: {position}
Here is the culture read of the swipe — what its audience recognizes, with receipts, why it lands and what must be kept: {culture_read}
Here is this avatar's culture map — what they watch and listen to, who they trust, their references and slang, what they care about, what they laugh at, and what is off-limits: {culture_map}

Plan the direct-response transformation. The lane, the opening beat and the
awareness entry are already decided and handed to you above — do not re-derive
them, do not overrule them. Your job starts after the opening.

**You can see the opening now, which is the point of this order.** The hooks
were written straight off the source before anyone decided where the product
goes. So place the product against the opening that actually exists, not
against an imagined one: find the beat the format itself re-engages attention,
and check it still works given how the hook set enters.

Two rules govern everything below:

**The product enters where the format already re-engages attention.** Not at
the end by default. Find the beat the source itself builds toward — an
escalation phrase, a payoff, a structural cut, a reveal, the resolution of a
bait or a question.

**Two runtimes, and confusing them is the mistake this section exists to
stop.** The *source* runtime is how long the thing we tore down runs. The
*asset* runtime is how long the thing we are building runs. They are only
the same number when the source is already a finished ad.

When the source is an organic post, it is a hook — it is short because it
was made to stop a scroll on someone's feed, not to sell anything. It
becomes the **opening** of our asset, and the rest of the asset does not
exist yet. So the source's seconds budget the opening, and the asset's
runtime is whatever the climb below needs.

**The climb decides the asset runtime, not the source.** Count the rungs the
message has to carry — problem, mechanism, proof, offer — and budget the
seconds they need.

**The mechanism rung is the brand's, and the market stage prices it.**
`{position}` names `MECHANISM`, `MARKET STAGE` and `LEADS WITH`. At *claims
exhausted* or *the mechanism is in the market*, the mechanism is the rung the
whole climb exists to reach — budget it real seconds, and place it where the
format re-engages attention, not in the close. At *first in the market* or *the
claim is in the market* it is a supporting beat. At *exhausted field* it is
dropped and the climb runs on identification; say so.

**The LINE has a beat.** Name the beat, by timestamp in the injected version,
where the position's `LINE`, its `SPINE`, or its `MECHANISM` in `MECHANISM NAME`'s
words lands — the injection pass may have printed `LINE HAS NO BEAT`, and this
is the pass that gives it one. It is normally the product-entry or
problem-entry beat or the rung right after it. If the format has no beat that
can carry it, that goes in THE RISK, in those words; you do not invent a beat.

**Every piece carries the product, at every rung and on both lanes. That
is not a question to ask.** (the owner, 2026-08-26, and 2026-10-20: "Our organic
and paid content should be indistinguishable.") A swiped organic post is raw
material for a piece for our brand; it does not matter that the post itself
sells nothing, because a post that sold something would not be organic. Never
reserve a slot asking whether the asset carries a product, an end card, an
offer or a destination, and never let the answer default to "no" while a
question waits. It carries a product. Budget for it.

**The rung is how funny the piece is, never whether it sells.** The rung is
decided by the HUMOR RUNG line below, read off the culture read and the
culture map. At rung 1 and 2 the joke rides on the problem and the product
arrives as the answer to it; at rung 3 and 4 the product is the punchline.
Every rung carries the product's entry, its claims and the close. **A rung 0
piece plays straight**, and the verdict decides where recognition happens
exactly as it always has. (This replaces the 2026-10-06 rule that let a rung
1–2 piece run as an organic post with the product left out: an organic piece is
the same video as the paid one, and only the lane above changes its ask.)

**On a hook source, at every rung, budget the whole climb from the start.** The source's
seconds buy you the opening and nothing else. Behind it sit the beats that
turn a scroll-stopper into an ad — naming the problem, giving it context,
opening the opportunity, the product as the answer, the close. A budget of
"the source plus two or three seconds" is this section failing: it prices
the opening and forgets the ad. Reserve real seconds for each rung, and let
the total be what the climb costs — for a short post turned into a direct
response asset that lands far closer to **thirty to sixty seconds** than to
the source's runtime.

An asset that carries one rung and earns a click is a real answer when that
is deliberately the plan; it is never the answer that falls out of a short
source by accident. If a nine-second post becomes a nine-second asset,
nothing was built.

Give me these four sections.
**2. PRODUCT PLACEMENT**

The beat where the product enters, quoted from the teardown record, and what
makes it that beat.

Under it, one labelled line: `LINE BEAT: <timestamp> — <which of LINE / SPINE /
MECHANISM lands there>`, or `LINE BEAT: none — <why the format cannot carry it>`.

If the format has no such beat, say so — some formats make no promise and
cannot carry a pitch; they are openings that hand off to another asset.
That is a real finding for the owner, and the chain still completes.

**5. THE RUNTIME BUDGET**

**This number is a GUIDE for the passes after you, never a ceiling** (the owner, 2026-09-25: "with time budget that's irrelevant, that's too limiting, it doesn't matter").
A needed beat is never cut or skipped to fit it, and a pass that lands over
it simply says so. Still budget the whole asset, — the passes downstream add body beats *and* an objection
line *and* a close, and a budget that covers only the body guarantees an
overshoot nobody chose. Give it as a small ledger:

- the source runtime;
- seconds reserved for body additions, and at which beat they land;
- seconds reserved for the objection line;
- seconds reserved for the close — and if the spec marks the source's
  ending device fixed or load-bearing, the close is a continuation *after*
  that device, so its seconds are additional, not swapped in;
- the total the finished asset may reach.

Reserve a beat's seconds only if the transformation plausibly needs the
beat — a reserve for every imaginable addition is the doubling rule
wearing a ledger. Say plainly if the budget cannot carry the rungs; a
format that cannot fit its message inside a runtime the format survives
is a real answer, and it is yours to give, not the later passes' to
discover.

**6. THE DEVICE CHECK**

The format's central device — the physical action or gimmick that makes it
work — read against the objection bank.

A device can contradict a brand as hard as a sentence can, and it will not
show up as a claim. Destroying a product on camera in front of a customer
whose live objection is that they do not get enough of it is a device
problem, not a copy problem. Name every collision, cite the file and rule
it collides with, and say whether the format survives without the device.

**Collisions are reported, never ruled on.** However many devices collide,
however load-bearing they are, your output is the report — the chain
completes every pass, the collisions ride forward in the flags, and the
owner decides what runs, what gets rewritten, and what dies. You do not
stop the chain, and you do not editorialize about whether the format
deserves to run. DECIDED 2026-08-18 (the owner): the machine executes and
flags; only a human kills a run.

**7. THE RISK**

The one thing most likely to go wrong in this specific asset. A weak
placement beat, a runtime too short for the climb, a format with nothing to
build toward.

Write no script lines. The next pass does that.

---

Renumber them 1–4: **1. PRODUCT PLACEMENT**, **2. THE RUNTIME BUDGET**,
**3. THE DEVICE CHECK**, **4. THE RISK**. The lane, opening beat and awareness
were handed to you — restate each in one line at the top so the plan is
readable on its own, then do not revisit them.

---

## THE VERDICT LINE — the first line of your output

Before anything else, print one line, exactly one of these two shapes:

```
PRODUCT ENTRY: 0:14 — the cut into the wide shot
PROBLEM ENTRY: 0:03 — "<the problem> out" over the wide shot
```

There is no third shape. A swipe whose own beats neither show a product nor
name a problem still gets an entry: the beat this pass ADDS, tagged `[ADDED]`,
printed `PROBLEM ENTRY: <time> — [ADDED] <the beat>` (or `PRODUCT ENTRY` when
the added beat is the product itself), and the climb is built from it. Every
piece carries the product, on both lanes (V1, 2026-10-20).

Then, on the second line:

```
HUMOR RUNG: 2 — product as a prop · the bottle sits on the sink in the wide shot, nobody mentions it
```

HUMOR RUNG: 0 straight · 1 problem only · 2 product as a prop · 3 product is the punchline · 4 full ad wearing the meme
0 is the default. A piece leaves 0 only when the swipe itself is funny, or the culture read names a meme or format this audience already laughs at and the problem fits in it. A testimonial, a before/after, a founder story, a demonstration and any piece about pain, health or money stays at 0 unless the source itself joked. The rung sets how the joke wears the product, never whether the product is there: every rung carries the product, its claims and the close. The lane, not the rung, decides the last ask.

A straight piece prints `HUMOR RUNG: 0 — straight · <the culture read's FUNNY OR
STRAIGHT line, or what in the swipe keeps it straight>`.

Read the rung off the culture read (its FUNNY OR STRAIGHT line, what any joke
rests on and what breaks it) and the culture map (what this avatar laughs at
and what is off-limits). A joke's reference must come from the culture read or
the avatar's culture map. Pick the rung the swipe and its culture read actually
support. When nothing supports a joke, the rung is 0.

**At rung 0 the verdict stands on its own,** exactly as before the rung existed:
`PRODUCT ENTRY` or `PROBLEM ENTRY`, whichever the beats below support. At
rung 1–4 the rung and the verdict must agree: `PROBLEM ENTRY` ≈ rung 1–2 ·
`PRODUCT ENTRY` ≈ rung 3–4. If stage 1b
recommended a rung, say whether you agree, in the same line. A rung 1–4 that
disagrees with the verdict is a finding — print it in THE RISK.

Then the four sections.

**The question is not "where does the product go".** It is **"where does the
viewer cross from watching into recognising themselves"** — the entry onto the
awareness ladder. A product appearing is one way that happens. It is not the
only way, and on organic sources it is usually not the way.

- **`PRODUCT ENTRY`** — the product itself enters at that beat and the asset
  can carry it.
- **`PROBLEM ENTRY`** — the beat where the problem gets named, shown or
  admitted, with no product in frame yet. The product still enters after
  it, at the beat this pass names — the piece is never left at the problem. The viewer has been
  stopped, has read the hook, and now sees their own problem stated. The
  selling continues from there, or hands to the destination. **Do not write
  `NO ENTRY` because a product cannot sit at that beat.**
- **There is no `NO ENTRY`.** When the injected version names nothing and
  shows nothing, the entry is a beat you ADD (`[ADDED]`) — the problem named,
  or the product itself — and the piece climbs from there to the product and
  the close. A piece that stops a scroll and names nothing is not a finished
  piece on either lane (V1).

**The mistake this line exists to prevent, in full.** On one run this
pass was asked only about product placement. It answered, correctly, that no
beat could hold a product — the reveal's job is to say *this is fine*, and a
fix there contradicts it. But the injected version of that same beat reads
**"<the problem> out"**: the problem, named on screen, at 0:03, on the longest
hold in the piece, immediately after the hook has done its work. That is a
textbook problem entry and a strong setup. Reported as "no placement", it
nearly turned a workable ad into a hand-off.

So: look for the product beat. If there is none, look for the beat where the
problem is named — in the injected version, not the source. If neither
exists, add the beat that names the problem (`[ADDED]`) and enter there.

---

## EXAMPLES ARE EXAMPLES

Every specific in this prompt — an age, a gender, a garment, a body part, a
product category, a named person — is **illustrative only**. None of it
describes the asset in front of you. Read an example for the shape of the rule,
then apply the rule to what you actually have.

The subject may be any age, any gender, anywhere, selling anything. This chain
runs the same on one person on a beach, someone talking to camera in a parked
car, two people in a kitchen, or a pair of hands on a countertop. Nothing in this prompt
assumes which.

**Who is on camera comes from the teardown record, never from this prompt.** If
the teardown says a person in their fifties in a parked car, that is the
subject — every rule below applies to them unchanged, and any pronoun in an
example above is about that example, not about the person in front of you.

**And the variables are agnostic too.** An example may be reworked freely — it
is illustrative and always was. But nothing that gets filled in at run time
may name a brand, a product or a category: brand detail arrives only through
the variables above, resolved from that brand's own folder. A rule that
cannot be written without naming the brand belongs in the brand folder, not
in this prompt. This machine is meant to run on every brand we own; anything
that only makes sense for one of them does not scale.


## THE TARGET AWARENESS — Variation video runs only

TARGET AWARENESS: {target_awareness}
SECTIONS THIS LEVEL CARRIES: {branch_sections}

If the two lines above are empty, this is not a Variation video run: skip
this section, and everything above is the whole job.

If they are filled, the source is a proven ad of ours and the baseline
injection is its own script, unchanged — VERSION 0 of the whole variation
tree. This run is ONE awareness level of that ad: the target above. Only
awareness moves. The format, the product, the cast and every part of the
structure the sections line does not touch stay exactly as the baseline has
them.

What changes for placement, and nothing else:

1. **The hook set's pick is this leaf's opening.** Place against it.
2. **The product enters where the target level allows.** Read the target's
   row in the awareness levels above — where the ad may begin and what it must
   not do. The product enters no earlier than that row allows.
3. **The runtime budget's rows are the sections this level carries.** Give
   each listed section its share; a section not listed gets none. The budget is
   still a guide, never a ceiling.
4. The VERDICT LINE and the HUMOR RUNG line are printed exactly as above.

## PART 2 — EXPANSION: the rules for this part

Here is the doctrine read of the source as swiped — its humor, delivery, register, reference-world and mood lines included: {doctrine_read}
Here is the culture read of the swipe — what its audience recognizes, with receipts, why it lands and what must be kept: {culture_read}
Here is this avatar's culture map — what they watch and listen to, who they trust, their references and slang, what they care about, what they laugh at, and what is off-limits: {culture_map}
Here is the target awareness for this run — filled only on a Variation video run, empty everywhere else: {target_awareness}
Here are the sections this awareness level carries, from the awareness map — filled only on a Variation video run, empty everywhere else: {branch_sections}
Here are the five awareness levels, each with what it must and must not do:
{awareness_levels}

Today is: {today}
Here is real language selected for this stage: {language}
Here is the replication spec — the construct of this script: {replication_spec}
The placement plan is your PART 1 above, in this same answer.
Here is the hook set — every hook shipping to test: {hook_set}
Here is the baseline injection: {baseline_injection}
Here is the teardown record: {teardown_record}
Here is the product: {product_file}
Here is our language bank: {language_bank}
Here is our offer: {offer_file}
Here are the body techniques, what each is for and the sub-methods each
carries: {techniques}
Here are the sections an argument is built from, and the techniques that
build each one: {sections}
Here are the five sophistication stages, with what each one leads with:
{sophistication_stages}
Here is what the research gatherer found for this run, with a receipt on
every claim: {research}
Here is our brand's story — who tells our version and what happens to them: its SPINE, TELLERS, STORIES with their beats and who each fits, and, once stage 1b has run, the story and teller this run picked (THIS RUN'S STORY), as labelled slots; `[UNFILLED]` when the brand has no story yet: {story}

**The language above is QUERIED, not dumped.** These rows were chosen
because of what they ARE — the kind of thing this stage needs — and ranked by
topic fit, how loud the row was, and how well sourced. Each is a real sentence
a real person said, carrying who said it and where it came from.

Use their words. Weigh the provenance: a row flagged `ATTRIBUTION ASSUMED` is
weaker evidence, and a paid panel is not a customer speaking — never present
either as something a customer said. If nothing in the rows fits, say so; an
empty result is a fact about the bank, not a licence to write the sentence
yourself.

**The bank is evidence, not a script. Interpret it; do not transplant it.**
Everything in it was true of a particular person, at a particular moment, in a
particular conversation — none of it was written for the asset you are making.
Re-judge every line before using it: does it fit what this is about, who is
speaking, and when this runs. **Keep the truth, drop the frame.**

**A month must be the real one.** Today's date is at the top and it is data,
exactly like the avatar and the banks. The banks record months because real
people name them — but they recorded when the speaker spoke, not when this
runs. Do not inherit theirs; substitute ours. A genuine historical reference — a decade,
an age, "when the kids were small" — is a fixed point and stays.


Add the body of the ad. Four moves are available. **None of them are
automatic.**

**The spec is the construct — read structure, never re-derive it.** The
replication spec already names the skeleton phase by phase, every
element's mandate, and where injections belong. Every beat you add names
the spec phase it lands in and the element mandate it serves; an added
beat that serves no mandate is decoration, and a beat placed by feel when
the spec names the slot is drift. The teardown record is reference for
scenes, rhythm and what was in frame — never for structure and never for
words.

**One body, many heads.** The hooks are the test cells; the body you
write is shared by all of them. Build it from the control opening, and
hold its register to the format's as the spec records it, not to any one
variation. Then check the fit: a hook the body cannot follow cleanly is a
compatibility defect — flag it, do not bend the body toward it.

Four rules govern everything below:

**The baseline is the only source of lines.** THE SCRIPT SO FAR copies
every baseline line **character for character** — every price, every name,
every piece of punctuation. Reaching past the baseline into the teardown
reintroduces the source's brand, numbers and category. The test is
mechanical: before you finish, compare every `[SOURCE]` row against the
baseline injection line it came from. **Any difference — one digit, one
word — means either the tag is wrong or the line is.**

**When the source is a hook, the body is your job and it is most of the
asset.** A short organic post makes no claim, names no product and carries
no offer — that is what a hook is. So "the source does not do this" is
never a reason for us not to do it. On a source like that the source can
only tell you how the asset *opens*; everything after the opening comes
from this pass, and four moves all answering "not needed" means an ad with
no product in it. That happened on 2026-08-26: a nine-second post came out
of this pass as an eight-second asset with nothing added but one card.

Hold the source's *structure* exactly. Do not hold its *silence*.

### What to build when the source has no product in it

A hook with no product is not a dead end and never a finished piece — it is a
starting rung, and the body is the climb to OUR product (the owner, 2026-08-26;
2026-10-20, V1: an organic piece is never a piece without the product). Read what the hook already puts in
the viewer's head, then build up from it in this order:

1. **Name the problem the hook implies.** The hook shows a state; it never
   says what is wrong. An opening that shows a mark and a covered-up
   wardrobe is a person living with something. Say what that something
   costs them — plainly, in their words.
2. **Give it context.** Why it happens, how long it has gone on, what they
   already tried. This is the part that earns the rest: a viewer who has
   not agreed there is a problem will not care about a solution.
3. **Open the opportunity.** Something changed, or something exists that
   they did not know about. Not the product yet — the possibility of one.
4. **Bring the product in as the answer to the problem you just built,**
   described, not merely named.
5. **Close** — what to do, and the offer if the files hold one.

Each of those is beats, and beats are seconds. **Budget them.** A hook that
opens at problem-awareness and never climbs is an ad that asks for nothing.

Where a rung genuinely cannot be built from the supplied files — no proof
exists, no offer is approved — say so in the ledger and climb the ones that
can. Skipping the climb entirely is never the answer.

**The placement plan's runtime budget is a guide, not a ceiling** (the owner, 2026-09-25: "with time budget that's irrelevant, that's too limiting, it doesn't matter").
Price every beat you add in seconds so the ledger is honest, but never
drop, shorten or skip a move this video genuinely needs to fit the number,
and never stop over it. Length is still earned one justified beat at a time
— add what the ad needs, and nothing it doesn't.

**A move earns its way in or it stays out.** For each of the four moves,
first name the baseline line that already does that move's job for this
video. **If that line exists, the move is not needed — substitution
already delivered it, and adding it again is blanket insertion.** A move
that does earn its way in must state, in one line, why this construct
cannot carry the core message without it, naming the spec element it
serves. A strong format arriving from stage 3 may need one move, or
none — that is the machine working, not you underdelivering.

**No number you were not given appears anywhere in your output.** Not in
the script, not in your commentary, not in the moves table, and not in the
slots list — a slot that reads `needs the customer count (e.g. "over
100,000")` has just written the number it exists to avoid, and the example
is what gets copied into the brief. This covers customer counts,
percentages, ratings, review totals, years in business, and prices.

The test is mechanical, because the urge to illustrate is strong enough to
survive being told not to: **no entry in your SLOTS section may contain a
digit, a quotation mark, or the string `e.g.`** Name the *kind* of fact in
plain words — "the number of customers who have bought it" — and stop
there.

**You are injecting, not rewriting.** The construct's spine — its
opening, its sequence, its device, its rhythm — survives this pass intact.
Every added beat is connective tissue that carries the core message the
placement plan names. None reorganise what is already there. If you find
yourself moving the spec's phases around, stop: that is a different job
and it destroys the reason the format worked.

The four moves, each behind the earns-its-way-in gate above.

**Social proof.** Only if nothing in the baseline tells the viewer anyone else
bought this *and* the construct has a phase where a stranger's experience
belongs. Never invent a number. If none is substantiated, write
`[SLOT: substantiated customer count]` and carry on.

**Describe the product, do not just name it.** Only if the baseline
introduces the product without saying what it is. When the source's winner
was a service and ours is a product the viewer has never seen, this is usually
the one move that is genuinely needed — one beat, in the avatar's words,
naming what it is and how it differs from what they have already tried.

**Give every component a benefit tied to the buying reason.** Only if the
baseline names components without jobs. If the baseline's substituted
lines already carry the mechanism inside the source's own beats, this move
is done and a component rundown on top of it is padding.

**Fix the mechanics the source got wrong.** The borrowed format describes
a usage that belongs to its own product, not ours — frequency, method,
duration, what the product visibly does. Take the documented usage from
the product file exactly as written and correct every mechanic carried
across. This is the move most often genuinely needed, because the script
reads fine either way — check it even when every other move stays out.

---

## THE DEPTH PASS — after the four moves, never instead of them

The four moves above are the gate and nothing here loosens it. A beat that
did not earn its way in through that gate does not earn it here either. This
pass is about the beats that ARE going in: what part of the argument each one
is doing, and which technique is available to build it well.

**Depth is an option, reached for where it genuinely makes sense — never a
rule forced on every beat.** A format that already works, carrying a message
it already carries, needs none of this. Reaching for a technique because the
list exists is exactly the padding the four-move gate was built to stop.

**THE SECTIONS THIS BODY CARRIES.** From the replication spec's skeleton and
the section list supplied above, name which sections this body actually
carries, in the order it carries them, using the section ids exactly as that
list spells them. A section the spec's skeleton has no phase for is not
carried — say so and move on. **Never add a section to the list.** If a phase
matches nothing on it, describe it plainly and mark it
`(candidate for the bank)`; that is a finding, not a licence to coin one.

**THE TECHNIQUE OPTIONS, PER SECTION.** For each section you named, the
section list already says which techniques build it. Print the section, the
techniques it maps to, and — for each technique you actually reached for —
the sub-method you used, named from the technique list above:

- **Intensification** — which of its enumerated ways this beat runs, and the
  fresh angle on the fulfilled desire it adds. Never the same picture twice:
  a repeat is not reinforcement, it is the viewer's attention leaking.
- **Identification** — which role the beat lets the viewer be seen playing,
  character or achievement, built from a role the avatar already claims.
  Never demand a jump the viewer has not been bridged to.
- **Gradualization** — the accepted fact the chain starts from, and each step
  from there to the claim. The opening fact is one the bank shows the avatar
  already believes, or the chain starts in mid-air.
- **Redefinition** — what is being renamed, and which drawback the renaming
  removes: too complicated, too small a thing to care about, too expensive.
- **Mechanization** — at the level the market's sophistication stage demands:
  named only · described · featured.
- **Concentration** — for the failed-solutions section: a named alternative
  and its sourced weakness, immediately followed by our fix for that exact
  weakness. **Never attack a weakness without supplying the fix in the same
  beat** — a bare attack reads as bias and buys nothing.
- **Camouflage** — the host format's own register, held. This one is a
  constraint on every beat rather than a beat of its own.

**MECHANISM LEADS AT STAGE 3 AND ABOVE.** Read the sophistication stage the
market state named. **At the third stage or higher, the mechanism — how it
works, not what it does — leads the body**, and a claim carried over from a
less sophisticated source reads as behind the market however well it is
written. At the first or second stage, a mechanism explanation is depth the
market has not asked for yet and usually costs more seconds than it earns.
Print the stage, and print what it means you led with.

**EVERY OPTION CARRIES A RECEIPT.** An option is used only where a language
bank row or a research receipt supports it. **Cite the row or the receipt
inline, or leave the beat clean.** Not "the avatar values X" — the row, or
the receipt line from the research above, quoted. Where the research came
back `[UNFILLED]`, the options it would have supported are simply not
reached for; an unfilled question is a clean beat, never a guess dressed as
a finding. A technique named without a receipt is a technique invented, and
the audit's receipt test fails it.

**The depth is never forced.** A body that carries three sections with two
techniques between them, every one receipted, is a correct output. A body
that carries seven techniques, three of them uncited, is a worse asset that
looks like more work.

---

Two things apply throughout. **The added beats must read as one continuous
thought**, not a format with a pitch bolted on — write the connective tissue
in the speaker's own rhythm, finishing sentences the language bank shows they
have already started. And **every message beat gets a shot**; a message with no
scene is a line the editor has to invent a picture for. Take shots from what
the source already does where you can.

Give me these eight sections.

**1. THE SCRIPT SO FAR**

Control opening through the end of the product section, beat by beat with
timings. A reader must be able to see the original still standing inside
it. Tag every beat, and the test is exact: **`[SOURCE]` means the words
are carried through unchanged from the baseline injection.** If you
altered so much as the usage instruction inside it, it is `[ADDED]`. Each
`[ADDED]` beat also names its spec phase and the element mandate it
serves.

**2. SCENE PER BEAT**

One line each: what is in frame.

**3. THE FOUR MOVES**

For each: the baseline line that already does its job, or the one-line
reason this construct needs it and the spec element it serves · what you
did · the line it produced, with its cost in seconds. "Not needed" with
the line that proves it is a complete and correct answer.

**4. THE DEPTH LEDGER**

The sections this body carries, in order. Per section: the technique(s)
reached for with their sub-methods, the receipt for each — a bank row or a
research line, quoted — and the beat it produced. A section with no technique
reached for says `clean` and that is a complete answer. End with one line: the
sophistication stage, and what it meant this body led with.

**5. HOOK COMPATIBILITY**

One line per hook in the set, control included: does the body's first
source beat follow it cleanly — same problem, same register, no dangling
setup? `FITS` or `BREAKS` with the reason. A `BREAKS` is a flag for the
human, never a licence to rewrite the body or the hook.

**6. THE RUNTIME LEDGER**

Arithmetic, on the page: the source runtime, the budget's reservation for
body additions, each added beat with its seconds, and the landing total.
If the total is over the guide, say by how much — nothing is cut for it.

**7. SLOTS**

Every `[SLOT: …]`, what fact would fill it, and who has that fact.

**8. WHAT THE SOURCE KEPT**

The construct's spine, phase by phase against the spec's skeleton. If any
phase was lost or moved, say so — that is a defect, not a choice.

Do not write the objection line or the call to action. The next pass does
those.

---

## THE CONNECTIVE TISSUE HAS TO SOUND LIKE A PERSON

Added at v5. On an organic source there is no commercial spine to inherit, so
this pass writes the lines that carry a viewer from their world into the product.
Those written-from-nothing lines are the ones that come back sounding like an
advert read aloud, and one flat line is enough to make the whole take feel
staged.

The test: **read the line out loud in the speaker's register. If you would not say it to
someone standing in your kitchen, it does not go in.**

What that rules out, specifically:

- **Marketing connectives.** "That's when I discovered", "here's the thing",
  "little did I know", "and that's why", "the secret is", "game changer".
  Nobody says these. They are the seam showing.
- **The pivot sentence.** A line whose only job is to turn the video toward the
  product — "so when I found something that actually worked…". If the turn
  needs a sentence to explain itself, the turn is in the wrong place. Move it.
- **Explaining the joke.** On a comedy source, the funny beat lands and then a
  line arrives telling the viewer what it meant. Cut that line every time.
- **Stacked adjectives.** "Rich, creamy, luxurious." Real speech uses one, and
  usually a plain one.
- **Symmetry.** "Not just X, but Y." "It's not about A. It's about B." Written
  rhythm, not spoken rhythm.
- **Reading the product's own words back.** A benefit lifted off the product
  file, spoken as though the speaker thought of it.

What it sounds like instead:

- **Half-sentences and restarts.** People stop and change direction mid-thought.
  A line that reads slightly broken usually plays as real.
- **The specific over the general.** Not "I was tired all the time" — "I fell asleep in
  the car park before I'd even started the engine."
- **Understatement at the moment of the claim.** The strongest line in a real
  video is usually the flattest one.
- **The avatar's own words first.** The language bank is verbatim customer speech. Use a
  phrase from it rather than writing a new one that means the same thing.

**One check before this pass ends:** find the line where the video turns toward
the product. Read it aloud. If it sounds like an advert, rewrite it as the
thing the speaker would actually say, or cut it and let the turn happen visually.

---

## EXAMPLES ARE EXAMPLES

Every specific in this prompt — an age, a gender, a garment, a body part, a
product category, a named person — is **illustrative only**. None of it
describes the asset in front of you. Read an example for the shape of the rule,
then apply the rule to what you actually have.

The subject may be any age, any gender, anywhere, selling anything. This chain
runs the same on one person on a beach, someone talking to camera in a parked
car, two people in a kitchen, or a pair of hands on a countertop. Nothing in
this prompt assumes which.

**Who is on camera comes from the teardown record, never from this prompt.** If
the teardown says a person in their fifties in a parked car, that is the
subject — every rule below applies to them unchanged, and any pronoun in an
example above is about that example, not about the person in front of you.

**And the variables are agnostic too.** An example may be reworked freely — it
is illustrative and always was. But nothing that gets filled in at run time
may name a brand, a product or a category: brand detail arrives only through
the variables above, resolved from that brand's own folder. A rule that
cannot be written without naming the brand belongs in the brand folder, not
in this prompt. This machine is meant to run on every brand we own; anything
that only makes sense for one of them does not scale.

---

## Before you hand this over

Run these and **print the result of each**. A check whose result is not
written down did not happen, and a principle in a bullet list does not bind —
this is the same rule stated as work.

**Fix first, in the copy itself, then report.** You cannot go back and edit
what you already wrote, so what you print above must already be corrected. A
fix described under an uncorrected line leaves the wrong words where people
read from.

**1 · TIME.** Name every month, season and holiday you wrote. Beside each,
confirm it agrees with the date at the top. A month that is not the current
one is a defect unless it is plainly historical — substitute the real one and
report it fixed. "The bank said July" is not a defence.

**2 · SOURCE.** For every concrete specific — an age, a number, a duration, a
named behaviour, a quoted line — name the file it came from **and tag what
kind of language it is**: `VERBATIM` (a real person's recorded words) ·
`AVATAR` (a documented behaviour in the profile's phrasing) · `ANGLE` (a line
a marketer drafted) · `FILE` (a product or offer fact). These files record the
difference deliberately; flattening it presents a copywriter's invention as
something a customer said. Anything untraceable is removed or rewritten.

Then one line: how many specifics are `VERBATIM`, and which lines carry
`ANGLE` language. A piece resting mostly on `ANGLE` is not disqualified — it
is a piece whose realness has not been demonstrated, and whoever reads it
should be told that plainly.

**THE STORY'S MIDDLE.** Where you expand what was tried, why it failed and what
turned, expand THIS RUN'S STORY from `{story}`: what the teller tried is its
`REASON TO SWITCH`, why it could not work is the gap `DISPLACES` names in the
position, and the turn is its `TURN`. The teller's own words come from the story's
receipts or the language rows above, never written for them. If `{story}` is
`[UNFILLED]` or no story was picked, expand as before.


## THE TARGET AWARENESS — Variation video runs only

TARGET AWARENESS: {target_awareness}
SECTIONS THIS LEVEL CARRIES: {branch_sections}

If the two lines above are empty, this is not a Variation video run: skip
this section, and everything above is the whole job.

If they are filled, the source is a proven ad of ours and the baseline
injection is its own script, unchanged — VERSION 0 of the whole variation
tree. This run is ONE awareness level of that ad: the target above. Only
awareness moves. The format, the product, the cast and every part of the
structure the sections line does not touch stay exactly as the baseline has
them.

What changes for the expansion, and nothing else:

1. **This stage runs even when the source is ALREADY AN AD.** On a Variation
   video run the sections change, and this is where they are built.
2. **The script carries exactly the sections listed — no more, no fewer.**
   Print, before section 1: `SECTIONS CARRIED: <ids, in order>`.
3. **A listed section the baseline does not carry is built**: to its
   definition in the sections above, with the techniques that list names for
   it, in the language rows' words, where the target level's order needs it.
   Every added line still names the baseline line or the section it serves —
   the gate on every move is unchanged.
4. **A section the baseline carries that is not listed is cut.** Print
   `DROPPED: <section id> · <why the target level does not call for it>` for
   each one.
5. Nothing the target level must not do (the awareness levels above) appears
   anywhere in the script.


## LANGUAGE USED — the last thing you print

LANGUAGE USED:
- <row id> | <the words as they appear in what you wrote>

One line for every language row above whose words you carried into what you
wrote. The id is the one in [brackets] beside the row. A row you read and did
not use is not listed. If you used none: `LANGUAGE USED: none`. The machine
reads this block to mark those rows as used in this asset, so the next run
reaches for words we have not said yet — list only what you actually used.


## THE VIBE HOLDS

This is social media. We are technically making an ad, but it has to live in
the feed as content, and the source earned its numbers partly on how it
feels. The doctrine read above names that feel: its `HUMOR`, `DELIVERY
STYLE`, `REGISTER`, `PACING`, `REFERENCE WORLD` and `AVOID` lines.

Print, before anything else you write:

`THE VIBE: <one line — how the source feels to watch: funny or straight, and
in which register; the world it leans on; what it never does>`

Then hold it in every line you write:

1. **A funny source stays funny.** Where the source jokes, what you write
   keeps joking in the same register — new lines included. Humor stays out of
   the proof and the offer beats only where the source itself played those
   straight.
2. **A straight source stays straight — unless the room plants the joke.**
   Straight is the default. A straight source stays straight unless the culture read or the culture map gives a receipt AND the piece is not a testimonial, a before/after, a founder story, a demonstration, or about pain, health or money.
   A straight source may take a planted joke only when it has a receipt: a meme, format or reference the culture read or the avatar's culture map shows this audience already laughs at. Name the receipt. A joke the writer invented is still a finding.
   Print each planted joke as `PLANTED JOKE: <the line> · <receipt, quoted
   from the culture read or the culture map>`. A planted joke never sits on the
   proof or the offer, and nothing on the culture map's off-limits line is
   planted.
3. **Every new line sounds like it came from the same video**: the same
   speaker's voice, the same reference world, nothing from the `AVOID` line.
   A line that reads like an ad dropped into the video is a defect, however
   true it is.
4. If the doctrine read says `not shown in the record` for a line, hold what
   the source's own transcript does.

**Feel versus structure.** Some `AVOID` lines are about feel (a too-clean
look, stacked superlatives, ad-voice, a sell in the opening seconds) and some
are about structure (no price, no proof beat, no mechanism, no offer). Feel
always holds. Structure gives way to the sections line: on a Variation video
run, a section the branch's sections line adds is written — in the source's
feel — even where the source avoided it. A price is still bound by the target
level's must-not.

**The mood and the one-of-a-kind line hold too.** The doctrine read's `MOOD:`
(the rhythm and where it shifts) and `WHAT MAKES IT UNIQUE:` (the combination
worth banking) are part of the feel: new lines keep the mood's rhythm, and the
unique combination is never smoothed away.


**If PART 1's reading says the lane is ALREADY AN AD**, the source already carries its structure: print `SKIPPED — ALREADY AN AD` under this part's marker and nothing else; PART 3 closes on the control hook plus the baseline injection's body.


---

=== PART 3: CLOSE ===

## PART 3 — CLOSE: the rules for this part

Here is the doctrine read of the source as swiped — its humor, delivery, register, reference-world and mood lines included: {doctrine_read}
Here is the culture read of the swipe — what its audience recognizes, with receipts, why it lands and what must be kept: {culture_read}
Here is this avatar's culture map — what they watch and listen to, who they trust, their references and slang, what they care about, what they laugh at, and what is off-limits: {culture_map}
Here is the target awareness for this run — filled only on a Variation video run, empty everywhere else: {target_awareness}
Here are the sections this awareness level carries, from the awareness map — filled only on a Variation video run, empty everywhere else: {branch_sections}
Here are the five awareness levels, each with what it must and must not do:
{awareness_levels}

The script so far is your PART 2 above (or, when PART 2 was skipped, the control hook plus the baseline injection's body).
The placement plan is your PART 1 above.
Here is the baseline injection: {baseline_injection}
Here is the replication spec: {replication_spec}
Here is the product: {product_file}
Here is our objection bank: {objection_bank}
Here is our offer: {offer_file}
Here are the close rules — the guarantee, and how a price is anchored:
{offer_close}
Here are the verification rules — what proof is, and where it is allowed to
land: {verification}

Finish the ad. Two moves, both short, neither restructuring anything.

**This pass layers. It never rebuilds.** Every beat you did not write this
pass is copied **character for character** from the script so far — same
words, same prices, same timings shifted only by what you insert. You add
at most two beats and move nothing. If a line upstream looks wrong to you,
flag it; correcting it here silently is how two passes end up fighting
over the same script.

**Pre-empt the objection sitting in the script.** Read the script as the
customer, start to finish, and find the flinch — the hesitation the script
creates and never answers. It is nearly always already documented in the
objection bank. The tell is that you feel resistance reading a line and the
script moves straight past it.

Write one line, in the speaker's voice, that kills it. Not a disclaimer, not
a paragraph — one line that sounds like the person talking. Place it
immediately after the beat that raises it; an objection answered three beats
late has already cost the click. If the script raises more than one, handle
the one that stops the most people and list the rest in the flags.

**Name the flinch before the offer.** The objection line is written at the
moment of the viewer's flinch — the hesitation they just felt, named aloud in
their own words as the objection bank records them. The guarantee-led close
answers that exact hesitation. The offer block drops the ambience and holds
the voice clear — never fake scarcity (the never-pair rules below still
hold). A close that answers a different objection than the one the viewer
flinched at is a close that was written for someone else.

**Redefine the objection the bank names.** An objection is often not argued
away, it is renamed — and the bank says which one is live here. Three shapes,
matched to what the objection actually is: *too complicated* is answered by
relabelling the process in ordinary words the avatar already uses; *too small
a thing to bother with* is answered by connecting it to the larger outcome it
sits inside, or by showing it is constant rather than occasional; *too
expensive* is answered by changing what the price is compared against, never
by defending the number. Name which one you used and quote the bank row it
answers. A redefinition with no bank row behind it is a reframe of an
objection nobody raised.

**Close with the offer — through the source's ending, not over it.** Check
the replication spec before touching the final beat. **If the spec marks
the source's ending device fixed or load-bearing, that device stays.** An
engagement ending the format depends on is part of why the video worked;
killing it is restructuring, and the previous version's instruction to do
so was wrong. Instead, the offer arrives as a **continuation the device
hands into**: the source asks its question, and the speaker answers it
themselves — what they ended up doing — and the offer lives inside that answer.
The engagement bait fires, then the sale lands.

Only when the spec does not protect the ending — the source just trails
off, or its CTA is platform housekeeping with no structural job — do you
replace it outright with the offer.

Either way the offer itself comes from the offer file: price, what is
included, the guarantee, the ask.

**A rule in a supplied file is not negotiable.** If the offer file forbids
something, remove it and report the removal. Never keep a known violation
and defer the decision into a flag, and never argue that it is acceptable
under some condition. Flags are for things you cannot resolve — a named rule
is not one of them.

**An upstream `[SLOT: …]` is still empty**, whatever the prose around it
says. If an earlier pass named an example figure in its commentary, that
figure does not exist. Carry the slot forward unfilled.

Four rules on the offer, all hard. The guarantee carries the risk, not
urgency. Quote the guarantee exactly as the offer file records it — never
paraphrase, soften or strengthen it. Obey every never-pair rule that file
sets. And never state a price it does not confirm; if the brand sells at
more than one price, say which one this is.

Keep the close to the length the format can carry. A close that runs longer
than the beat before it reads as a different video.

There is an optional move, and it is not part of every pass. **Restructure
who speaks** when the asset would be materially stronger with a different
narrator, or when geography is a targeting advantage the supplied files
support. Keep the source format's best device when you do — the narrator
changes, the thing that made the format work does not. Produce it as a
separate variation rather than an edit, so both survive and can be tested
against each other.

**The variation obeys every rule the main script obeys** — same claim
limits, same never-pair rules, same ban on invented numbers and
unsubstantiated results. A variation written outside the rules is not a
variation, it is a second problem.

Give me these eight sections.

**1. THE OBJECTION LINE**

The flinch, quoted from the script · the objection it maps to in the bank ·
the line, in voice · where it goes.

**2. PROOF PLACEMENT**

Proof lands where the reader already demands it, not where it is convenient
to put it. Read the script start to finish and name the beat where doubt
peaks — the moment the viewer is most likely to stop believing. Then name the
proof that lands there, from the supplied files only: a stat, a test, a
testimonial, an authority, a visible result. Quote it, and say which beat it
sits in.

Proof front-loaded because it is proof is proof spent before it was wanted.
And proof copy is still selling copy — short, specific, dramatic. A dry fact
in the middle of a script in voice is a seam the viewer hears.

If the files carry no proof for the beat where doubt peaks, write
`[SLOT: proof at the doubt beat]` and say what kind of proof would fill it.
Never invent one, and never promote a claim to a proof by restating it.

**3. THE ANCHOR**

**A price is never stated bare.** Name the anchor this price is set against —
what the viewer would otherwise be spending, over what period, on what — and
write the comparison as one line in voice. The anchor comes from the offer
file or the product file; if neither carries one, write
`[SLOT: price anchor]` and leave the price un-anchored rather than inventing
a comparison. Never anchor against a competitor's price unless a supplied
file states it.

A price cut needs its own reason to be believed. If the offer file carries a
discount, name the believable reason it is cheap, from the file. A discount
with no reason behind it reads as the product being worth less.

**4. THE GUARANTEE**

The guarantee is the last intensification beat, not a legal footnote:
**every promise the script made, restated once more, wrapped inside the risk
reversal.** List the promises the script made, in order, then write the
guarantee beat that carries them — with the guarantee sentence itself quoted
exactly as the offer file records it, never paraphrased, softened or
strengthened. The promises are restated around that sentence; the sentence
itself is untouched.

If the offer file records no guarantee, say so plainly and write no
substitute. An invented guarantee is the most expensive line in the chain.

**THE SUPPLY MATH — the guarantee becomes an instruction.** Work out, from
the offer and product files only, what the offer physically gives the buyer
and how long it lasts: units in the offer × amount per unit ÷ (amount per use
× uses per week) = how long the supply runs. Set that against the guarantee's
window. **The whole offer is used inside the guarantee window**: when the offer
file rules the routine that does that (how often, how much each time), use it
word for word; otherwise work it out — offer ÷ uses in the window = amount each
time — and print it. Then write the guarantee beat as **exactly what the buyer does**: how
often, for how long, with how much of what they bought — so the viewer hears
the routine that the guarantee covers, and the offer's size answers "will it
last". The guarantee sentence itself is still quoted exactly. Print the math
in one line, each figure with its file. A figure the files do not carry is
written `[RESEARCH: what is needed]` — the machine researches it after this
pass — and never estimated.

**5. THE CLOSE**

Whether the spec protects the ending device, in one line. Then, **on the paid
lane**, the offer, written — as a continuation of the device if protected, as a
replacement if not. **On the organic lane there is no offer:** after the remedy,
the product's turn (what it is and what it does, in one or two spoken lines, as
bold as the files allow) and then the lane's ask, word for word — nothing else.
No bundle or multi-buy, no dosing or usage schedule, no guarantee text, no
label-style statistic, wherever the offer file has them (V23, the owner 2026-10-21:
"nothing sterile at all"). The four rules on the offer, the supply math and the
guarantee count below are the paid lane's; on organic print `ORGANIC — no offer
stack` in their place.

**Keep the bite.** Every line this pass writes or touches keeps the injection's
enemy, its visible result and its hook at the injection's shock level. A body
line that names the problem more mildly than the injection did is a defect, not
a polish.

**THE LAST ASK IS THE LANE'S.** The last thing said, and the last thing on
screen, is the ask THE LANE above names, and only that ask. **Paid:** tap the
button and buy — the platform's own button, in words the offer file allows
(where the file says no link, the button carries the click and nothing names a
URL). **Organic:** comment the word the lane line names (the viewer gets the
link back), or follow — never a button, never a URL. The source's engagement
device may still fire before it — a comment ask earlier in the video is a good
re-hook, especially in a story or a creator-style piece — but the last ask is
the lane's. Everything before the last ask is the same piece on both lanes;
organic only lets the story carry a little longer before the first claim.
Where an offer file still says "the ask is the click" for every piece, that
line is read as the PAID lane's rule (a change to scope it is with the brand
file's owner).

**No timecodes in this section.** The words and the staging only. THE FULL
SCRIPT is the one place a beat gets a time, because a brief that reaches a shoot
carrying two timelines for the same beat is a hazard that surfaces on the
day, and the maker has no way to tell which one is real.

**6. THE FULL SCRIPT**

Hook to close, beat by beat, each marked `[SOURCE]` or `[ADDED]`, with the
scene per beat. Every beat this pass did not add must match the script so
far exactly.

End with one line: the source's runtime, the runtime the placement plan
expected, and where this script actually lands — three numbers, nothing
else.

**If your total is past the plan's expected runtime, say so in FLAGS and
say by how much.** Do not quietly trim beats to hit the number and do not
pretend the number was met. A format that ran forty seconds does not
survive being briefed at ninety, and the maker is the one who finds that
out on set — so the overshoot is the finding, and a human decides whether
to cut a move or accept the longer cut.

**7. FLAGS**

Every rule in the product or offer file this script brushes against, every
claim without substantiation, every unfilled slot, every objection left
unhandled, every upstream line you believe is wrong. Never invent a number
to close a gap.

**8. VARIATION**

If the optional move was used, the alternate version, kept whole and
separate.

---

## Before you hand this over — print this

Four checks. Run them, print the result of each, and fix what fails before
you return anything. The rules they enforce are already stated above, and
being stated is not enough — a rule only binds when its result is written
down.

**THE GUARANTEE COUNT.** (Paid lane only; on organic the count is zero — no
guarantee text at all.) Quote the guarantee sentence exactly as the offer
file records it. Then list **every** sentence in this script that promises
money back, refunds, or anything returned — in any wording, however
glancing. If the list has more than one entry, the extras are paraphrases:
delete them and print the corrected list.

A paraphrase drops the conditions the exact wording carries. Placed before
the real one it is worse than wrong — the viewer hears an unconditional
promise attached to the price, then hears it narrow as the speaker keeps
talking, which reads as the offer shrinking in front of them.

**THE ONE-TIMELINE CHECK.** Name the section that carries the timecodes.
Then confirm, section by section, that no other section carries any. If one
does, delete its times — do not reconcile the two, because reconciling
leaves two sets that agree today and drift on the next pass.

**THE SUPPLY CHECK.** (Paid lane only — on organic print `ORGANIC — no offer stack`
and confirm no bundle, dosing schedule, guarantee text or label-style statistic is
anywhere in the script.) Print the supply math line. Then quote the guarantee
beat and say whether it tells the viewer what to do, how often, for how long,
and with how much of the offer. If it does not, rewrite it and print it again.

**THE LAST-ASK CHECK.** Quote the last spoken line and the last on-screen line,
and the lane. Say whether each makes the lane's ask — paid: the click; organic:
comment the word, or follow. If either asks for anything else as the final
ask, rewrite it and print it again. Then confirm the product, its claims and
the close are in the piece whichever the lane.

Print every result. A check whose result is not written down did not
happen.

## THE REACH CHECK (V24, 2026-10-21)

(the owner, 2026-10-21: "what makes them stand out on feeds is the absurdity. it has nothing to do with just claims and brand approval … we need to maximize reach / impressions, the shock value absurdity and hyper specific depictions are what make AI content acceptable".) Add one line to your checks: `REACH` — `HELD` or `FAILED: <the line or picture that would not stop a
thumb, and the swipe's matching moment>`. It FAILS when the script's hook, demonstration or depictions are less
absurd, less shocking or less specific than the swipe's matching moments, or when a line was softened for brand
taste. A FAILED REACH counts toward the VERDICT. Never fail or soften a script for being too bold, too gross or too
absurd; no other check of yours may hold a line for tone or brand approval (the factual checks stay: product,
ingredients, structure, the ask).
