*v14 (2026-09-19, the owner: "storytelling and intent over volume" — a storytelling swipe): reads `{story}`, the brand's story file beside its position. Adds THE STORY TEST: at least one hook opens the teller's story, in an OPENS IN moment, through the story's ENTRY.*

**The language above is QUERIED, not dumped.** These rows were chosen
because of what they ARE — the kind of thing this stage needs — and ranked by
topic fit, how loud the row was, and how well sourced. Each is a real sentence
a real person said, carrying who said it and where it came from.

Use their words. Weigh the provenance: a row flagged `ATTRIBUTION ASSUMED` is
weaker evidence, and a paid panel is not a customer speaking — never present
either as something a customer said. If nothing in the rows fits, say so; an
empty result is a fact about the bank, not a licence to write the sentence
yourself.

**The bank is evidence, not a script. Interpret it; do not transplant it.**
Everything in it was true of a particular person, at a particular moment, in a
particular conversation — none of it was written for the asset you are making.
Re-judge every line before using it: does it fit what this is about, who is
speaking, and when this runs. **Keep the truth, drop the frame.**

**A month must be the real one.** Today's date is at the top and it is data,
exactly like the avatar and the banks. The banks record months because real
people name them — but they recorded when the speaker spoke, not when this
runs. Do not inherit theirs; substitute ours. A genuine historical reference — a decade,
an age, "when the kids were small" — is a fixed point and stays.


Write the opening. Six versions — a control and five variations, five
different TAKES on the same ad. **All of
them ship to test.** The human gate on this pass is approval, not
selection: nothing is picked, nothing is ranked, and the body written
after this pass must carry any hook in the set. The variations are the
test cells; the market decides.

**A hook is five things, printed as five labelled slots: its TAKE (the angle
in a few words), its LINE (what is said), its CARD (the words on screen in the
first second), its SCROLL (the scroll stopper) and its THUMB (the thumbnail —
the still that sells the video in the grid, with its words).** The line is what the speaker
hears or reads; the scroll stopper is the first-frame visual event that
arrests the thumb before a single word lands. Neither is optional and
neither is the other's caption — they are the same thought in two
channels, show and tell at once. "Selfie walk-and-talk at the entrance"
is a camera setup, not a scroll stopper. A scroll stopper is a thing
*happening*: an act mid-motion, an object where it should not be, a
texture close enough to feel, a before mid-transformation. If the frame
would read as ordinary with the sound off, it stops nobody. Take the
visual vocabulary from what the source already does where it serves;
build inside the format's world where it does not.

**The spec already did the abstraction — read it, do not redo it.** The
replication spec names the opening phase, its duration and its fixed
bounds in the structural skeleton; the element inventory carries the
opening element's mandate; the script architecture names the premise and
its job. **That is the foundational hook, and it is read out of the spec,
cited, never re-derived from the teardown.** The teardown record is for
what was in frame; the reading supplies the awareness entry. If
the spec is missing any of this, that is a stage-2 defect — flag it and
stop rather than deriving around it.

**The reading names the opening beat by timestamp.** Rewrite that
beat and no other. Do not rewrite a later line because it looks more like a
hook.

**THE OPENING POINT — when `{opening_at}` names a line, it overrides the
reading.** the owner has cut the ad to start there. That line is the opening beat:
the control is that line as the source says it, and everything before it is
gone from the ad — no version may lean on a beat that no longer plays. The
format below is found from THAT line, not from the source's first line. When
`{opening_at}` says `none`, the reading's opening beat stands.

**One brief, one discrete problem.** The reading and the baseline
pin the problem this asset solves. Every hook names that problem, in the
avatar's own dominant words for it — the words the research shows the avatar
uses most, not the category's default synonym. A strong research moment
that names a *different* problem is not a hook variation; it is a
different brief. Write no hook on it — route it to the BRIEF VARIATION
OPPORTUNITIES section, one line, and move on. Hooks that shift the
problem mid-set do not widen the test; they contaminate it.

**THE UNAWARE ENTRY — when the reading's entry level is `unaware`, the rule
above changes, and only then.** An unaware reader is not thinking about the
problem while they scroll. A hook that names it, shows it, or shows the tools
of its category is addressed to somebody who is not there yet — they scroll
past it as an ad for a thing they do not shop for. So at this entry level:

- **The problem still pins the brief — as the DESTINATION.** It is where the
  body arrives, a rung at a time. It is never in the opening. Do not name it,
  do not show it, do not point the camera at the part of the body or the
  room where it lives.
- **Every one of the six openings is a portrait.** It enters on the person:
  something they do, say, avoid, laugh at, or are proud of — a habit, a
  place, a ritual, a running joke among their people — that they would recognise
  as themselves in under a second, with nothing of ours in frame. The source is
  entertainment they already watch; the opening belongs to THAT world, and
  the teardown record's opening beat tells you what world it is.
- **What may not be in the line OR the frame of an opening:** the symptom,
  the word for it, a close-up of it, the products or tools of the category
  (ours or anyone's), a before/after, a count of things tried, a complaint
  about results. Any of these makes it a problem-aware hook wearing an
  unaware label.
- **"Every hook traces to a verbatim" still holds — pull from different
  rows.** Use what the avatar says about their life, their people, their routines
  and how they carry themselves. A row that is about the problem is not
  available to an opening at this level; if the bank offers nothing else, say
  so — that is a finding about the bank.
- **The variations still differ** — each enters through a different part of
  their life: five different moments, five different first frames, five
  different thumbnails. Five portraits of the same person, never one portrait
  with a word swapped.

**Version 0 is the straight swipe, and it is the control.**

Take the source's opening beat exactly as it is and change only the words
that must change — the brand, the product, the category. Nothing else.
Same sentence, same rhythm, same length, same punctuation. Its scroll
stopper is the source's own first frame, held.

This is the version that already performed, wearing our name. Every variation
is a bet against it in the same test, and if none of them beat it that is
a real finding rather than a failure. **Label it clearly as the control
and never improve it.**

Then write five variations. The rest of this prompt is about those.

Five rules govern what replaces the source's words:

**Every hook traces to a verbatim.** Pull the moment from the language bank
and the avatar — a behaviour, a time of day, an object, something done
with the hands. The strongest hooks name a private moment the avatar thought nobody
had noticed. A hook that cannot cite its source is invented and gets cut,
however well it reads. Reaching for a phrase that is not in the bank is the
signal you are writing at the avatar instead of from them.

**Finish a sentence the avatar has already started.** The bank holds
half-phrases they use about themselves. A line lands when it completes one they recognise
rather than introducing a new one.

**The line and the scroll stopper must be about the same thing.** A
strong line over an unrelated first frame kills the script in the first
second — the words say one thing, the picture says another, and the viewer
resolves it by scrolling. The stopper shows the thing the line names,
already happening.

**A variation is its own take — a different way into the same ad.** The
control carries the source's format word for word (THE FORMAT LOCK below);
the five variations do not repeat it with a word swapped. Each one opens the
same body from a different place: a different moment, a different line, a
different picture in the first frame, a different thumbnail. Two variations
that a viewer would read as the same opening are one variation. What every
take keeps from the format is its WORLD — the speaker, the register, the
reference the culture read names, the place the body continues in — so the
body that follows can carry any of the six.

**You know who this is for. Use it.** Triage named the avatar, the funnel and
the subject; the bank holds what that person actually says. An opening that
would work for anyone in this category has ignored all of it. The specific,
slightly uncomfortable, true thing is what stops a scroll — the safe, general
thing is what every competitor is already running.

**Dramatize.** The strongest opening is a real moment at its sharpest, not a
statement about a problem. Something done, hidden, caught, or said out loud
that people usually keep quiet. Discreet in what it claims; dramatic in what
it shows. A moment can be shown; a category cannot.

**The control's format is fixed; the takes are not.** The control is the
source's shape — see THE FORMAT LOCK. Every take is built from the language
rows and from the script's own context, never invented to be different.

What stays banned is the ad-shaped template the source did NOT use: "If you're struggling
with…", "If you're tired of…", "If you've ever…", "POV:", "Nobody talks
about…", "Here's why…", "The one thing…", "Stop scrolling if…". They signal
an ad in the first two words and none of them is how a person starts a
sentence. If the source opened on one of them, you may keep it — that is the
source's move and version 0 already carries it. Otherwise never introduce
one. The source's own format is not one of these — it is the swipe, and it is
kept. The test is the fills: if a hook's fills could be reused verbatim by a
different brand in a different category, they are generic — refill them from
the bank. **Print before writing:** what the source's opening was doing, in one
phrase, as the context you are working from.

**Match the speaker's own voice.** Sentence length, vocabulary, rhythm, and
any quirks the teardown record shows. If the speaker drops articles, the hook
drops articles. Where the source's shape leaves the choice open, a behaviour
beats a condition — a behaviour is how the avatar talks about their life, a
condition is how a brand talks about them.
Every hook must also stay inside the format's own register as the spec
records it — the body that follows is shared, so a hook whose register the
body could not follow is out of the set, however strong the line.

Write each hook in the format's own currency, per the reading: a
spoken line if the format speaks, a card if it does not, a frame if it has
neither.

Give me these four sections.

**1. THE FOUNDATIONAL HOOK**

Read from the spec, with citations: the opening phase and its bounds
(structural skeleton) · the opening element's mandate (element
inventory) · the premise and its job (script architecture) · the
awareness entry (the reading). Nothing here is your derivation.

Then print THE FORMAT, exactly as THE FORMAT LOCK below says — three labelled
lines, `FORMAT LINE:`, `FORMAT CARD:` and `FORMAT FRAME:`.

**2. VERSION 0 — THE CONTROL**

The source's opening, swiped straight, with only brand words changed. Show
the source line and ours side by side so the swap is visible, then its
scroll stopper — the source's own first frame, held. No citation
needed — it is the source's own line. Then its labelled lines, exactly as
THE FORMAT LOCK says: `TAKE V0: control` · `LINE V0:` · `CARD V0:` (the
source's own card, or `none`) · `SCROLL V0:` (the source's own first frame) ·
`THUMB V0:` (the source's own cover frame and words, or its first frame).

**3. THE FIVE VARIATIONS**

For each, five labelled lines, one per line and nothing else on the line:
`TAKE Vn:` — the angle, in a few words (the moment of their life it enters
through) · `LINE Vn:` — what is said, in the speaker's voice, on one line ·
`CARD Vn:` — the words on screen in the first second, never `none` on a
variation unless the opening is wordless and has no card (lines separated by " / ") · `SCROLL Vn:` — **the first-frame event,
a thing happening, concrete enough to shoot from the sentence alone, and not
the control's frame with one detail changed** · `THUMB Vn:` — the thumbnail:
the one still, what is in it and where, and its words. Then: every specific
in the take and the verbatim it came from **quoted inside its original full
sentence**, with its source · what it assumes about the speaker · how the
take hands over to the body's next beat.

Quoting the whole original sentence is not decoration. A fragment can be
cited accurately and still be used to mean something the customer never
said — showing the sentence it lived in makes that visible on the page.

That last one matters. If the speaker cannot truthfully say it, the hook is
unusable no matter how good it is. Flag it plainly.

**4. BRIEF VARIATION OPPORTUNITIES**

The strong seams you did NOT hook because they name a different problem
than this brief's. One line each: the seam · its verbatim · why it is a
different brief rather than a hook. No script lines, no development —
these are notes for a future teardown or research pass, not work product.
If there were none, say none.

Write no other part of the script.

---

## THE OPENING, READ FIRST (v23)

Before you print a format, read what the swipe's opening actually is. Look at
the first three seconds in the verbatim transcript, the frame map and the
layer sheet, and print one line:

`OPENING: spoken` — a person says real words in the first three seconds.
`OPENING: card` — no one speaks, but words are on screen in the first second.
`OPENING: wordless` — no real words are said and none are on screen. Music, a
sound effect, a sigh, a "Hmm.", laughter, or background chatter the
transcript flags as heard differently all count as no words.

Then one line on **what stops the scroll** in the source: the first picture,
the first motion, the sound, the gag. That is the thing every version has to
match in strength, whatever the opening is.

**A wordless opening is fine. It is the format, not a gap.** Do not invent a
spoken line, do not turn a sound into a sentence, and do not put a caption on
a swipe that has none unless the variation needs one to stop the scroll.
For a wordless opening:

- `FORMAT LINE: none` and `FORMAT CARD: none` (or the source's card, if it
  has one). No blanks, so no ELEMENTS lines for the line.
- `LINE V0` … `LINE V5` all print `none`. `CARD Vn` prints `none` when the
  swipe has no card, or its own words when the swipe has one.
- **The take lives in `SCROLL` and `THUMB`.** Every one of the five is a
  different first-two-seconds event: a different picture, a different first
  motion, a different thing caught on camera, or a different sound cue. Five
  scroll stoppers that are the same shot with one prop changed are one take,
  and the machine holds them.
- Build each scroll stopper from the avatar's own moments and words, the same
  way you would build a spoken line: quote the full sentence it came from.

For a spoken or card opening, everything below applies as before.

---

## THE FORMAT LOCK

The source's opening worked for a reason, and the reason is its shape. This
pass does not reinvent it. It finds the shape, names its blanks, and fills
them.

**1 · Find the format — from the source's own words.** Take the source's
opening line exactly as the teardown record's verbatim transcript has it (the
line the reading names). Keep every word as it is, and put a named blank in
`[CAPITALS]` over only the words that carry something the brief changes — the
place, the problem or desire, the person's own specifics (an age, a name, a
count), the product, the category. The spec's script architecture already
names these blanks for the opening beat; use its names. **Never add a blank
the spec did not name, and never rewrite a fixed word** — not even to the
spec's own paraphrase of it. **A blank covers only the words that change**: a
word every version keeps (the noun after a verb that changes, say) is fixed,
outside the blank. Print:

`FORMAT LINE: <the source's line, word for word, with its blanks>`

Do the same for the opening's hook card (the words on screen in the first
beat, lines separated by " / "), and for the first frame (the teardown's
description of it: camera, place, what is happening — the variable parts in
blanks):

`FORMAT CARD: <the source's card, word for word, with its blanks>`
`FORMAT FRAME: <the source's first frame, with its blanks>`

If the source has no card, print `FORMAT CARD: none`, and every `CARD` line
says `none`.

**2 · The control fills the blanks with the swap and nothing else.** Version
0 is the format with each blank filled by what the rewrite already put there
— the baseline injection's opening beat. That is the swipe wearing our name.

**3 · The format binds the control; the takes keep its world.** Only VERSION
0 is held to the fixed words. The five variations are five takes (section 3):
each keeps the format's WORLD — the speaker, the register, the reference, the
place the body continues in — and every specific in it comes from the
language bank, the avatar, the brand files or the cast, cited in its full
original sentence. **Across the five, no two share a take, a first-frame
event or a thumbnail.**

**4 · Every blank is a named element, and elements hold each other.** A
blank is not free-standing: it is an element of this script, and the script's
other elements depend on it. Name every element the opening touches — the ones
in its blanks AND the ones the body brings in that a blank must agree with (a
person the body puts in a place, a count the card states, a thing the body
treats). Then write, for each blank, what it must hold, **in element names
only**: `[PLACE] holds [EXPERT] — the body puts [EXPERT] with [SUBJECT] inside
it` · `[AGE] = [SUBJECT]'s age in the cast` · `[PROBLEM] = the problem the body
treats`. A fill is right only when every element it must hold is true of it:
a place where [EXPERT] could not be is wrong however good it sounds. The
source's own fill shows the kind: keep that kind, change the instance.
Print, above the variations:

`ELEMENTS:` — one line per element: `[NAME] · what it is in this script ·
what it must hold or match (other element names) · where its fill comes from
(bank · cast · brand file · research)`

These names are the injection points. Every later pass, and every future run
of this format, fills the same elements.

**5 · A blank the files cannot fill is researched, not invented.** When a
fill needs a fact that is not on file — a real place name, a real number, a
real price — write `[RESEARCH: what is needed, in a few words]` in that
blank and move on — naming what the element must hold, so the research looks
for the right kind of thing (not "a real [PLACE] nearby" but "a real [PLACE]
nearby that holds [EXPERT], as the ELEMENTS line says"). The machine researches every one of those after this pass,
from the brand's files and then the web, and writes the fact in with its
receipt. A fill you made up to avoid a research marker is the defect this
rule exists for.

**6 · The scroll stopper and the thumbnail.** The control's first frame is
the source's, held. Each take's first frame is its own event, showing what
its line says, already happening — inside the format's world (the same room,
the same people) but a different thing happening in it. Each take's thumbnail
is its own still, chosen to make that take's promise in the grid; never the
same frame as another take's.

**What this lock does not touch:** the tests below still apply to what goes
into the blanks — the rung, the stage, the story, the portrait rule at an
unaware entry. A fill that breaks one is replaced with a fill that does not;
the format is never broken to pass a test. If a test cannot be passed by any
fill, print that as a flag about the format against our market and leave
the format standing.

**Print the lines the machine reads, exactly this way, one per line and
nothing else on the line:** `FORMAT LINE:` · `FORMAT CARD:` · `FORMAT FRAME:`
in section 1; `TAKE Vn:` · `LINE Vn:` · `CARD Vn:` · `SCROLL Vn:` · `THUMB Vn:`
for V0 to V5 in sections 2 and 3. The machine checks `LINE V0` and `CARD V0`
against their `FORMAT` (every fixed word present, in order), checks every
variation printed all five slots, and holds any variation whose line, card,
scroll stopper or thumbnail reads as another version's with words swapped.

---

## EXAMPLES ARE EXAMPLES

Every specific in this prompt — an age, a gender, a garment, a body part, a
product category, a named person — is **illustrative only**. None of it
describes the asset in front of you. Read an example for the shape of the rule,
then apply the rule to what you actually have.

The subject may be any age, any gender, anywhere, selling anything. This chain
runs the same on one person on a beach, someone talking to camera in a parked
car, two people in a kitchen, or a pair of hands on a countertop. Nothing in
this prompt assumes which.

**Who is on camera comes from the teardown record, never from this prompt.** If
the teardown says a person in their fifties in a parked car, that is the
subject — every rule below applies to them unchanged, and any pronoun in an
example above is about that example, not about the person in front of you.

**And the variables are agnostic too.** An example may be reworked freely — it
is illustrative and always was. But nothing that gets filled in at run time
may name a brand, a product or a category: brand detail arrives only through
the variables above, resolved from that brand's own folder. A rule that
cannot be written without naming the brand belongs in the brand folder, not
in this prompt. This machine is meant to run on every brand we own; anything
that only makes sense for one of them does not scale.

---

## Before you hand this over

Run these and **print the result of each**. A check whose result is not
written down did not happen, and a principle in a bullet list does not bind —
this is the same rule stated as work.

**Fix first, in the copy itself, then report.** You cannot go back and edit
what you already wrote, so what you print above must already be corrected. A
fix described under an uncorrected line leaves the wrong words where people
read from.

**1 · TIME.** Name every month, season and holiday you wrote. Beside each,
confirm it agrees with the date at the top. A month that is not the current
one is a defect unless it is plainly historical — substitute the real one and
report it fixed. "The bank said July" is not a defence.

**2 · SOURCE.** For every concrete specific — an age, a number, a duration, a
named behaviour, a quoted line — name the file it came from **and tag what
kind of language it is**: `VERBATIM` (a real person's recorded words) ·
`AVATAR` (a documented behaviour in the profile's phrasing) · `ANGLE` (a line
a marketer drafted) · `FILE` (a product or offer fact). These files record the
difference deliberately; flattening it presents a copywriter's invention as
something a customer said. Anything untraceable is removed or rewritten.

Then one line: how many specifics are `VERBATIM`, and which lines carry
`ANGLE` language. A piece resting mostly on `ANGLE` is not disqualified — it
is a piece whose realness has not been demonstrated, and whoever reads it
should be told that plainly.

## THE JOKE LOCK

A funny opening works because of its joke, and the joke has a shape just like
the line does. Before filling any blank, read the doctrine read's `HUMOR:` line
and the record's opening beat, and print:

`THE JOKE: <none — or how it works, in one line: what is said, what is really
true, and the gap between them that makes it funny>`

Name the MECHANISM, not the words. Shapes a mechanism can take, as examples of
shape only: *an impossible, instant change stated with a straight face*;
*a comparison to a famous face nobody could literally become*; *a deadpan
understatement of an obvious disaster*; *a mock-serious warning about something
trivial*. If the HUMOR line is `none` and the opening plays straight, the
opening stays straight unless the room plants a joke. Straight is the default. A straight source stays straight unless the culture read or the culture map gives a receipt AND the piece is not a testimonial, a before/after, a founder story, a demonstration, or about pain, health or money. A straight source may take a planted joke only when it has a receipt: a meme, format or reference the culture read or the avatar's culture map shows this audience already laughs at. Name the receipt. A joke the writer invented is still a finding.
When the culture read or the culture map gives you one, print `THE JOKE:
PLANTED — <how it works, in one line> · <receipt, quoted from the culture read
or the culture map's humor section>` and every rule below applies to it as if the source had
made it, except that VERSION 0 stays the source's own straight opening. When
neither gives you one, print `THE JOKE: none` and skip this section. Nothing on
the culture map's off-limits line is ever planted.

When there is a joke:

1. **The joke is locked like a fixed word.** Every version, control included,
   carries the same mechanism. A version that loses it has broken the format,
   however true its fills are.
2. **Name the blank the joke lives in** — `JOKE BLANK: [NAME]` — the blank
   whose fill carries the exaggeration, the understatement or the reversal.
3. **Fill the joke blank with a fresh instance of the same joke — never a
   literal benefit.** An exaggeration is filled with another exaggeration of
   the same size; an understatement with another understatement. Keep: no joke on the proof or the offer. Allow: the twist beat may be the product doing its job, shown, not claimed. A plain beat with the proof always follows it.
   So the fill may be the product as the punchline — shown in the frame doing
   what it does — but never the product's result stated as a claim: stating it
   flatly turns the joke into a claim and the laugh is gone. When a fill puts
   the product in the twist, print under that version `PROOF FOLLOWS: <the
   body beat, by its label in the baseline, that proves it plainly>`; if the
   baseline has no such beat, write `PROOF FOLLOWS: [SLOT: plain proof beat
   after the hook]` so the body pass writes one. The hook's job is the laugh
   that makes the viewer watch the body to find out what is really true.
4. **The fill comes from the room, not the writer.** Take its reference world
   from the language rows (the famous faces, tropes and in-words the avatar's
   room already uses — rows tagged `taste`, `in-word`, `subculture` where they
   exist), from the doctrine read's REFERENCE WORLD line, from the culture
   read below and from the avatar's culture map (its humor section: laughs at · tropes · creators).
   Cite it like any other fill. A joke the room does not make is a joke about
   the room.
5. **The truth test still holds, one level down.** A joke is not a claim — an
   impossible exaggeration is heard as a joke, so it needs no proof. What must
   stay true is what the joke points at: the change the body shows. A joke
   fill that points at a change the body never shows is false, and is replaced.
6. **The five variations never use the same instance twice**, the way the main
   blank never takes the same fill twice.

---

## Before you hand these over — print this

Checks. Run them, print the result of each, and fix what fails before
you return anything. A rule stated in prose does not bind.

**THE FORMAT TEST.** Print the FORMAT LINE, then `LINE V0` under it. Say
whether every fixed word is there, in order, unchanged; if not, rewrite the
control into the format and print it again, corrected. Do the same for the
card. The variations are not held to the format — THE DIVERSITY TEST checks
them.

**THE TRUTH TEST.** For each of the six, take every fill and check it against
its ELEMENTS line: is every element it must hold true of it? Say so in one line
per fill, naming the elements. A fill that makes any later beat
false is replaced and printed again, corrected.

**THE DIVERSITY TEST.** Print a table, one row per version V0–V5: TAKE · the
first five words of the LINE · the SCROLL event in a few words · the THUMB in a
few words · the bank row behind it. Then read down each column. **Any two
rows a viewer would take for the same opening — the same moment, the same
first-frame event, the same thumbnail, or one sentence with a word swapped —
fail**: rewrite the later one as a different take, from a different row, and
print the corrected table. The control is the format; a variation that reads
as the control with a word swapped fails too.

**THE RUNG TEST.** The reading names the entry level and quotes that level's
`must_not`. Copy that quoted must-not here, then take all six openings —
control included — one at a time, and say whether it breaks it. **A hook that
names the price, the product or the direct claim earlier than its entry level
allows is a defect, not a style choice**, and it is the failure this test
exists for: the line reads perfectly well and is simply invisible to the
reader it was written for.

Two things this test is not. It is not licence to rewrite the control — if
version 0 breaks the must-not, that is a finding about the source's own
entry level against ours, printed as a flag and left standing. And it is not
a ban on the words themselves later in the asset; it is about where the
argument is allowed to BEGIN.

Any variation that breaks its entry level's must-not is rewritten to enter a
rung lower and printed again, corrected. Print the must-not, the six
verdicts, and every rewrite.

Print all four lists. A check whose result is not written down did not happen.

**THE PORTRAIT TEST — the sixth list, printed only when the entry level is
`unaware`.** For each of the six openings, control included, fill three slots:
`IN FRAME:` the single thing the camera is on · `IN THE LINE:` the noun the
sentence is about · `VERDICT:` portrait | symptom | category | product. Any
opening whose verdict is not `portrait` is rewritten to enter on the person
and printed again, corrected — the control is the one exception: it stays as
the source had it and carries the flag. An opening that shows the symptom
"because it is relatable" is the exact failure this test exists for.

**THE STAGE TEST — the fifth list.** The rung test says where the argument may
*begin*; this one says what it must *lead with*. `{position}` names the
`MARKET STAGE` and `LEADS WITH`. Copy both here. Then take all six openings,
control included, and say in a few words what each one leads with — a plain
claim, an enlarged claim, a mechanism, an enlarged mechanism, or identification
alone — and whether that is what the stage calls for. **An opening that leads
with something this market has already discounted is a hook the reader has
already scrolled past**, however well it reads: at *claims exhausted* a plain
promise is invisible; at *exhausted field* a mechanism is one more pitch.

The control is exempt from rewriting — if version 0 fails, print it as a
flag about the source's market against ours and leave it standing. A variation
that fails is rewritten to lead with what `LEADS WITH` names, built from the
same bank rows, and printed again corrected. A hook does not have to *say* the
mechanism to lead with it — a scroll stopper that shows the how counts; say
which channel carries it. Where the reader's dominant word for the problem
is in `PROBLEM WORD`, every hook uses it; a `NEVER` word in any opening is a
defect, fixed and reported.

Print the stage, its leads-with, the six verdicts, and every rewrite.

**THE JOKE TEST.** Print `THE JOKE:` again. If it is `none`, print `JOKE TEST:
none` and move on. Otherwise, for each of the six, one line: the joke-blank
fill · `LANDS` (the same mechanism, a fresh instance, from the room) or `FLAT`
(a literal benefit stated as a claim, a repeated instance, or the mechanism
lost) · why, in a few words. A fill that shows the product doing its job as the
twist LANDS only with its `PROOF FOLLOWS:` line printed. Every `FLAT` variation
is rewritten with a new instance and printed again, corrected. The control is
the source's own joke and stays as it is.

**A planted joke on a straight source.** If `THE JOKE:` reads `PLANTED`, print
`PLANTED JOKE RECEIPT:` with the line of the culture read or the culture map it
came from, quoted. A straight source may take a planted joke only when it has a receipt: a meme, format or reference the culture read or the avatar's culture map shows this audience already laughs at. Name the receipt. A joke the writer invented is still a finding.
A planted joke with no receipt is removed from every version and printed as
`FINDING: invented joke — <the joke>`; the variations go back to straight. The
control is the source's straight opening and is never given the joke.

**THE STORY TEST.** `{story}` names this run's story and its teller. At least one
hook in the set fills the format's blanks **from that story**: the teller's own
words for the problem, landing in one of the `OPENS IN` moments, in the teller's
register from `VOICE` — the format itself unchanged. It still passes every
other test above. Mark it `STORY OPENER` in the set. If `{story}` is `[UNFILLED]` or
no story was picked, print `STORY TEST: no story` and skip it.


## THE TARGET AWARENESS — Variation video runs only

TARGET AWARENESS: {target_awareness}
SECTIONS THIS LEVEL CARRIES: {branch_sections}

If the two lines above are empty, this is not a Variation video run: skip
this section, and everything above is the whole job.

If they are filled, the source is a proven ad of ours and the baseline
injection is its own script, unchanged — VERSION 0 of the whole variation
tree. This run is ONE awareness level of that ad: the target above. Only
awareness moves. The format, the product, the cast and every part of the
structure the sections line does not touch stay exactly as the baseline has
them.

What changes for the hooks, and nothing else:

1. **Open at the target level.** The six hooks open where the TARGET level
   may begin, not where the reading says the source enters. Read the target's
   row in the awareness levels above — what that viewer knows, what the ad
   must and must not do, where it may begin — and open there. Nothing the
   target level must not do appears in any of the six openings.
2. **The format lock holds for the control.** Find the FORMAT LINE, CARD and
   FRAME from the source exactly as the lock says. Every fixed word of VERSION
   0 stays; only its blanks are refilled — with what the target level's
   opening is about.
3. **VERSION 0 at this level.** When the target is the level the source enters
   on, VERSION 0 is the source's own opening, word for word. At any other
   level, VERSION 0 is the source's own fills moved to the target level with
   the fewest words changed that the level demands — the nearest version of
   the proven hook at this level.
4. **The five variations** are five different takes at the target level, every
   rule above unchanged.
5. **A level the format cannot reach.** If a fixed word itself does something
   the target level must not do, print `LEVEL CONFLICT: <the fixed word> · <the
   must-not it breaks>` directly under the FORMAT lines, write the six as close
   to the level as the fixed words allow, and never change a fixed word to get
   there. That conflict is the owner's call.
6. Print, directly under the heading of section 1: `TARGET LEVEL: <level>` and
   `SOURCE LEVEL: <the level the reading gives>`.


## LANGUAGE USED — the last thing you print

LANGUAGE USED:
- <row id> | <the words as they appear in what you wrote>

One line for every language row above whose words you carried into what you
wrote. The id is the one in [brackets] beside the row. A row you read and did
not use is not listed. If you used none: `LANGUAGE USED: none`. The machine
reads this block to mark those rows as used in this asset, so the next run
reaches for words we have not said yet — list only what you actually used.


## THE REFERENCE IS PART OF THE FORMAT (v19, 2026-10-05)

Here is the culture read of the swipe — what its audience recognizes, with receipts, why it lands and what must be kept: {culture_read}
Here is this avatar's culture map — what they watch and listen to, who they trust, their references and slang, what they care about, what they laugh at, and what is off-limits: {culture_map}

When the read names a reference (`CONFIRMED` or `LIKELY`) that the opening carries — the recognizable look in the first frame, a line the audience knows, the reveal of who it is — that reference is a FIXED element of the format, exactly like a fixed word: it stays in VERSION 0 and in every variation, in the same place and at the same moment. A blank never swallows it, and no version trades it for a generic stand-in or stops to explain it. The variations fill the blanks around it. When the read says `CULTURE LOAD: NONE` or `NO CULTURE READ`, this changes nothing.

## THE LANE — paid or organic, and it changes only the ask (2026-10-21)

{ask_lane}

Organic and paid are the same video. The owner, 2026-10-20: *"Our organic and
paid content should be indistinguishable. The only core difference is really
just the ask and the way that we gradualize that sales pitch."* So the lane
above changes exactly two things, and nothing else:

1. **The last ask.** Paid: tap the button below and buy. Organic: comment the
   word (the one word the lane line names), or follow. The last thing said and
   the last thing on screen is that ask, and only that ask.
2. **How gradually the pitch arrives.** Organic lets the story carry longer
   before the product's first claim; paid may bring it sooner. The ORDER of the
   argument never changes.

The job, the product, where the product enters and every claim are the same
on both lanes; only the close differs (below — V23). Claims stay as bold as the brand files allow on both
(the owner, 2026-10-20: "don't worry about over claiming at this point it's
content"). An organic piece is never a piece without the product, and a paid
piece is never a different video with a button added.

**On the organic lane the close is the turn and the ask — no offer stack** (the owner, 2026-10-21: "i'm going for
real binge watch content man, nothing sterile at all"). After the remedy, the product's turn and the lane's ask,
word for word, and nothing else: no bundle or multi-buy, no dosing or usage schedule, no guarantee text, no
label-style statistics ("<n> in 100…"). The product, the mechanism and the claims stay — as bold as the brand files
allow. The paid lane keeps the offer.

*v15 (2026-10-06, the owner: "i mean not every add is funny...." · "it's more about the foundational way to identify these cultural nuances"): STRAIGHT is the default. The HUMOR RUNG ladder starts at 0 · straight; the old instruction to pick the highest rung the joke survives is gone — placement picks the rung the swipe and its culture read actually support, and 0 when nothing supports a joke. A rung 0 piece follows the VERDICT LINE exactly as v13 did (PRODUCT / PROBLEM / NO ENTRY); the rung–verdict mapping applies to rung 1–4 only. Reads `culture_map` (the avatar's humor map is now a culture map; humor is one section of it) in place of `humor_map`. Everything in v14 stands.*

*v14 (2026-10-06, the owner: "not even a brand, not even a product sometimes. Maybe it's just even the problem"): humor rules 3 and 5. Reads `culture_read` and `humor_map`. Adds a HUMOR RUNG line right under the VERDICT LINE and maps the two: NO ENTRY ≈ rung 1, PROBLEM ENTRY ≈ rung 1–2, PRODUCT ENTRY ≈ rung 3–4. The "we are building a product ad" rule now holds for rung 3 and 4; a rung 1–2 piece is an organic post and may carry no product (research: prominent branding lowers sharing, Tellis 2019; the message lands in the plain beat after the gag, Yoon 2022). Everything in v13 stands.*

Here is a baseline injection — our brand substituted into a format that
performs: {baseline_injection}

Here is the teardown record it came from: {teardown_record}
Here is its replication spec: {replication_spec}
Here is the product: {product_file}
Here is our objection bank: {objection_bank}
Here is the reading of the source — its lane, its opening beat and its
awareness entry: {source_reading}
Here is the hook set that will open our version: {hook_set}
Here is our brand's declared position — its LINE, SPINE, MECHANISM, the MARKET STAGE it sells
into and what that stage LEADS WITH, as labelled slots: {position}
Here is the culture read of the swipe — what its audience recognizes, with receipts, why it lands and what must be kept: {culture_read}
Here is this avatar's culture map — what they watch and listen to, who they trust, their references and slang, what they care about, what they laugh at, and what is off-limits: {culture_map}

Plan the direct-response transformation. The lane, the opening beat and the
awareness entry are already decided and handed to you above — do not re-derive
them, do not overrule them. Your job starts after the opening.

**You can see the opening now, which is the point of this order.** The hooks
were written straight off the source before anyone decided where the product
goes. So place the product against the opening that actually exists, not
against an imagined one: find the beat the format itself re-engages attention,
and check it still works given how the hook set enters.

Two rules govern everything below:

**The product enters where the format already re-engages attention.** Not at
the end by default. Find the beat the source itself builds toward — an
escalation phrase, a payoff, a structural cut, a reveal, the resolution of a
bait or a question.

**Two runtimes, and confusing them is the mistake this section exists to
stop.** The *source* runtime is how long the thing we tore down runs. The
*asset* runtime is how long the thing we are building runs. They are only
the same number when the source is already a finished ad.

When the source is an organic post, it is a hook — it is short because it
was made to stop a scroll on someone's feed, not to sell anything. It
becomes the **opening** of our asset, and the rest of the asset does not
exist yet. So the source's seconds budget the opening, and the asset's
runtime is whatever the climb below needs.

**The climb decides the asset runtime, not the source.** Count the rungs the
message has to carry — problem, mechanism, proof, offer — and budget the
seconds they need.

**The mechanism rung is the brand's, and the market stage prices it.**
`{position}` names `MECHANISM`, `MARKET STAGE` and `LEADS WITH`. At *claims
exhausted* or *the mechanism is in the market*, the mechanism is the rung the
whole climb exists to reach — budget it real seconds, and place it where the
format re-engages attention, not in the close. At *first in the market* or *the
claim is in the market* it is a supporting beat. At *exhausted field* it is
dropped and the climb runs on identification; say so.

**The LINE has a beat.** Name the beat, by timestamp in the injected version,
where the position's `LINE`, its `SPINE`, or its `MECHANISM` in `MECHANISM NAME`'s
words lands — the injection pass may have printed `LINE HAS NO BEAT`, and this
is the pass that gives it one. It is normally the product-entry or
problem-entry beat or the rung right after it. If the format has no beat that
can carry it, that goes in THE RISK, in those words; you do not invent a beat.

**Every piece carries the product, at every rung and on both lanes. That
is not a question to ask.** (the owner, 2026-08-26, and 2026-10-20: "Our organic
and paid content should be indistinguishable.") A swiped organic post is raw
material for a piece for our brand; it does not matter that the post itself
sells nothing, because a post that sold something would not be organic. Never
reserve a slot asking whether the asset carries a product, an end card, an
offer or a destination, and never let the answer default to "no" while a
question waits. It carries a product. Budget for it.

**The rung is how funny the piece is, never whether it sells.** The rung is
decided by the HUMOR RUNG line below, read off the culture read and the
culture map. At rung 1 and 2 the joke rides on the problem and the product
arrives as the answer to it; at rung 3 and 4 the product is the punchline.
Every rung carries the product's entry, its claims and the close. **A rung 0
piece plays straight**, and the verdict decides where recognition happens
exactly as it always has. (This replaces the 2026-10-06 rule that let a rung
1–2 piece run as an organic post with the product left out: an organic piece is
the same video as the paid one, and only the lane above changes its ask.)

**On a hook source, at every rung, budget the whole climb from the start.** The source's
seconds buy you the opening and nothing else. Behind it sit the beats that
turn a scroll-stopper into an ad — naming the problem, giving it context,
opening the opportunity, the product as the answer, the close. A budget of
"the source plus two or three seconds" is this section failing: it prices
the opening and forgets the ad. Reserve real seconds for each rung, and let
the total be what the climb costs — for a short post turned into a direct
response asset that lands far closer to **thirty to sixty seconds** than to
the source's runtime.

An asset that carries one rung and earns a click is a real answer when that
is deliberately the plan; it is never the answer that falls out of a short
source by accident. If a nine-second post becomes a nine-second asset,
nothing was built.

Give me these four sections.
**2. PRODUCT PLACEMENT**

The beat where the product enters, quoted from the teardown record, and what
makes it that beat.

Under it, one labelled line: `LINE BEAT: <timestamp> — <which of LINE / SPINE /
MECHANISM lands there>`, or `LINE BEAT: none — <why the format cannot carry it>`.

If the format has no such beat, say so — some formats make no promise and
cannot carry a pitch; they are openings that hand off to another asset.
That is a real finding for the owner, and the chain still completes.

**5. THE RUNTIME BUDGET**

**This number is a GUIDE for the passes after you, never a ceiling** (the owner, 2026-09-25: "with time budget that's irrelevant, that's too limiting, it doesn't matter").
A needed beat is never cut or skipped to fit it, and a pass that lands over
it simply says so. Still budget the whole asset, — the passes downstream add body beats *and* an objection
line *and* a close, and a budget that covers only the body guarantees an
overshoot nobody chose. Give it as a small ledger:

- the source runtime;
- seconds reserved for body additions, and at which beat they land;
- seconds reserved for the objection line;
- seconds reserved for the close — and if the spec marks the source's
  ending device fixed or load-bearing, the close is a continuation *after*
  that device, so its seconds are additional, not swapped in;
- the total the finished asset may reach.

Reserve a beat's seconds only if the transformation plausibly needs the
beat — a reserve for every imaginable addition is the doubling rule
wearing a ledger. Say plainly if the budget cannot carry the rungs; a
format that cannot fit its message inside a runtime the format survives
is a real answer, and it is yours to give, not the later passes' to
discover.

**6. THE DEVICE CHECK**

The format's central device — the physical action or gimmick that makes it
work — read against the objection bank.

A device can contradict a brand as hard as a sentence can, and it will not
show up as a claim. Destroying a product on camera in front of a customer
whose live objection is that they do not get enough of it is a device
problem, not a copy problem. Name every collision, cite the file and rule
it collides with, and say whether the format survives without the device.

**Collisions are reported, never ruled on.** However many devices collide,
however load-bearing they are, your output is the report — the chain
completes every pass, the collisions ride forward in the flags, and the
owner decides what runs, what gets rewritten, and what dies. You do not
stop the chain, and you do not editorialize about whether the format
deserves to run. DECIDED 2026-08-18 (the owner): the machine executes and
flags; only a human kills a run.

**7. THE RISK**

The one thing most likely to go wrong in this specific asset. A weak
placement beat, a runtime too short for the climb, a format with nothing to
build toward.

Write no script lines. The next pass does that.

---

Renumber them 1–4: **1. PRODUCT PLACEMENT**, **2. THE RUNTIME BUDGET**,
**3. THE DEVICE CHECK**, **4. THE RISK**. The lane, opening beat and awareness
were handed to you — restate each in one line at the top so the plan is
readable on its own, then do not revisit them.

---

## THE VERDICT LINE — the first line of your output

Before anything else, print one line, exactly one of these two shapes:

```
PRODUCT ENTRY: 0:14 — the cut into the wide shot
PROBLEM ENTRY: 0:03 — "<the problem> out" over the wide shot
```

There is no third shape. A swipe whose own beats neither show a product nor
name a problem still gets an entry: the beat this pass ADDS, tagged `[ADDED]`,
printed `PROBLEM ENTRY: <time> — [ADDED] <the beat>` (or `PRODUCT ENTRY` when
the added beat is the product itself), and the climb is built from it. Every
piece carries the product, on both lanes (V1, 2026-10-20).

Then, on the second line:

```
HUMOR RUNG: 2 — product as a prop · the product sits on the sink in the wide shot, nobody mentions it
```

HUMOR RUNG: 0 straight · 1 problem only · 2 product as a prop · 3 product is the punchline · 4 full ad wearing the meme
0 is the default. A piece leaves 0 only when the swipe itself is funny, or the culture read names a meme or format this audience already laughs at and the problem fits in it. A testimonial, a before/after, a founder story, a demonstration and any piece about pain, health or money stays at 0 unless the source itself joked. The rung sets how the joke wears the product, never whether the product is there: every rung carries the product, its claims and the close. The lane, not the rung, decides the last ask.

A straight piece prints `HUMOR RUNG: 0 — straight · <the culture read's FUNNY OR
STRAIGHT line, or what in the swipe keeps it straight>`.

Read the rung off the culture read (its FUNNY OR STRAIGHT line, what any joke
rests on and what breaks it) and the culture map (what this avatar laughs at
and what is off-limits). A joke's reference must come from the culture read or
the avatar's culture map. Pick the rung the swipe and its culture read actually
support. When nothing supports a joke, the rung is 0.

**At rung 0 the verdict stands on its own,** exactly as before the rung existed:
`PRODUCT ENTRY` or `PROBLEM ENTRY`, whichever the beats below support. At
rung 1–4 the rung and the verdict must agree: `PROBLEM ENTRY` ≈ rung 1–2 ·
`PRODUCT ENTRY` ≈ rung 3–4. If stage 1b
recommended a rung, say whether you agree, in the same line. A rung 1–4 that
disagrees with the verdict is a finding — print it in THE RISK.

Then the four sections.

**The question is not "where does the product go".** It is **"where does the
viewer cross from watching into recognising themselves"** — the entry onto the
awareness ladder. A product appearing is one way that happens. It is not the
only way, and on organic sources it is usually not the way.

- **`PRODUCT ENTRY`** — the product itself enters at that beat and the asset
  can carry it.
- **`PROBLEM ENTRY`** — the beat where the problem gets named, shown or
  admitted, with no product in frame yet. The product still enters after
  it, at the beat this pass names — the piece is never left at the problem. The viewer has been
  stopped, has read the hook, and now sees their own problem stated. The
  selling continues from there, or hands to the destination. **Do not write
  `NO ENTRY` because a product cannot sit at that beat.**
- **There is no `NO ENTRY`.** When the injected version names nothing and
  shows nothing, the entry is a beat you ADD (`[ADDED]`) — the problem named,
  or the product itself — and the piece climbs from there to the product and
  the close. A piece that stops a scroll and names nothing is not a finished
  piece on either lane (V1).

**The mistake this line exists to prevent, in full.** On one run this
pass was asked only about product placement. It answered, correctly, that no
beat could hold a product — the reveal's job is to say *this is fine*, and a
fix there contradicts it. But the injected version of that same beat reads
**"<the problem> out"**: the problem, named on screen, at 0:03, on the longest
hold in the piece, immediately after the hook has done its work. That is a
textbook problem entry and a strong setup. Reported as "no placement", it
nearly turned a workable ad into a hand-off.

So: look for the product beat. If there is none, look for the beat where the
problem is named — in the injected version, not the source. If neither
exists, add the beat that names the problem (`[ADDED]`) and enter there.

---

## EXAMPLES ARE EXAMPLES

Every specific in this prompt — an age, a gender, a garment, a body part, a
product category, a named person — is **illustrative only**. None of it
describes the asset in front of you. Read an example for the shape of the rule,
then apply the rule to what you actually have.

The subject may be any age, any gender, anywhere, selling anything. This chain
runs the same on one person on a beach, someone talking to camera in a parked
car, two people in a kitchen, or a pair of hands on a countertop. Nothing in this prompt
assumes which.

**Who is on camera comes from the teardown record, never from this prompt.** If
the teardown says a person in their fifties in a parked car, that is the
subject — every rule below applies to them unchanged, and any pronoun in an
example above is about that example, not about the person in front of you.

**And the variables are agnostic too.** An example may be reworked freely — it
is illustrative and always was. But nothing that gets filled in at run time
may name a brand, a product or a category: brand detail arrives only through
the variables above, resolved from that brand's own folder. A rule that
cannot be written without naming the brand belongs in the brand folder, not
in this prompt. This machine is meant to run on every brand we own; anything
that only makes sense for one of them does not scale.


## THE TARGET AWARENESS — Variation video runs only

TARGET AWARENESS: {target_awareness}
SECTIONS THIS LEVEL CARRIES: {branch_sections}

If the two lines above are empty, this is not a Variation video run: skip
this section, and everything above is the whole job.

If they are filled, the source is a proven ad of ours and the baseline
injection is its own script, unchanged — VERSION 0 of the whole variation
tree. This run is ONE awareness level of that ad: the target above. Only
awareness moves. The format, the product, the cast and every part of the
structure the sections line does not touch stay exactly as the baseline has
them.

What changes for placement, and nothing else:

1. **The hook set's pick is this leaf's opening.** Place against it.
2. **The product enters where the target level allows.** Read the target's
   row in the awareness levels above — where the ad may begin and what it must
   not do. The product enters no earlier than that row allows.
3. **The runtime budget's rows are the sections this level carries.** Give
   each listed section its share; a section not listed gets none. The budget is
   still a guide, never a ceiling.
4. The VERDICT LINE and the HUMOR RUNG line are printed exactly as above.

## THE LANE — paid or organic, and it changes only the ask (2026-10-21)

{ask_lane}

Organic and paid are the same video. The owner, 2026-10-20: *"Our organic and
paid content should be indistinguishable. The only core difference is really
just the ask and the way that we gradualize that sales pitch."* So the lane
above changes exactly two things, and nothing else:

1. **The last ask.** Paid: tap the button below and buy. Organic: comment the
   word (the one word the lane line names), or follow. The last thing said and
   the last thing on screen is that ask, and only that ask.
2. **How gradually the pitch arrives.** Organic lets the story carry longer
   before the product's first claim; paid may bring it sooner. The ORDER of the
   argument never changes.

The job, the product, where the product enters and every claim are the same
on both lanes; only the close differs (below — V23). Claims stay as bold as the brand files allow on both
(the owner, 2026-10-20: "don't worry about over claiming at this point it's
content"). An organic piece is never a piece without the product, and a paid
piece is never a different video with a button added.

**On the organic lane the close is the turn and the ask — no offer stack** (the owner, 2026-10-21: "i'm going for
real binge watch content man, nothing sterile at all"). After the remedy, the product's turn and the lane's ask,
word for word, and nothing else: no bundle or multi-buy, no dosing or usage schedule, no guarantee text, no
label-style statistics ("<n> in 100…"). The product, the mechanism and the claims stay — as bold as the brand files
allow. The paid lane keeps the offer.

## THE SWIPE'S BITE — KEEP ITS SHOCK LEVEL (2026-10-21)

The owner, 2026-10-21: *"i'm going for real binge watch content man, nothing sterile at all"* · *"this script seems
much more tame."* Found on a run the same day: the swipe's "parasites hiding in your gut lining" came out as "<our
problem> hiding in <where it sits>" — the same sentence with the disgust taken out, and the reason anyone kept watching
gone with it.

So, for every line of the swipe that carries its bite:

1. **A scary, concrete thing maps to an equally concrete thing for our problem — never a mild, abstract word.**
   Name what our problem physically IS, at its most vivid and slightly gross true version: what it is made of,
   where it sits, what it does, what it looks like up close. "<the problem>" is the label; the bite is what the
   brand files and the avatar's own words say they are and do. A word a medical leaflet would use is the
   sterile version, and it is a defect.
2. **Name an ENEMY, in the swipe's own shape.** Where the swipe had a culprit (the parasites, the toxin, the
   thing hiding), ours has one too, said as plainly, sitting in the same place in the sentence.
3. **Name a VISIBLE RESULT, in the swipe's own shape.** Where the swipe promised something you can see or feel
   change, ours promises the thing a viewer would see change on themselves.
4. **Same shock level, never higher than the files can carry and never lower than the swipe.** Every fact still
   comes from the brand's files and the avatar's words; the bite is in which true thing you name and how
   concretely you name it. Claims stay bold (the owner, 2026-10-20: "don't worry about over claiming at this point
   it's content").

In the ELEMENT LEDGER, every line that carried the swipe's bite is a row: the swipe's words, ours, and `KEPT` (as
concrete and as charged) — a line marked softer is an Adapted change and goes to the owner.
